# Changelog — HelixDevelopment/HelixConstitution

## Unreleased — 2026-11-11 — v73.4 release preparation: the CM-KILLPG-PGID-GUARD regression fixed (7 unguarded sites on `fc57ad0c`), the PROVISIONAL Version-badge rule made case-insensitive, and a mechanical, resumable release cut (`scripts/release/`)

**Status of this entry: UNRELEASED; implemented and tested on host fixtures only; not VERIFIED-DONE (CON-226, Ready for testing, PROVISIONAL-DONE per §11.4.290).** Independent review of `ec58c0ae` (2026-11-11): GO-with-follow-ups, 0 critical, 0 important, 9 minor, 5 nit (`docs/qa/review_rel734c_2026_11_11.md`); follow-ups CON-227 to CON-234. Landed on `main` by merging `main` `72a4bebb` into the vehicle; at landing one new process-group signal text site from `main` (`scripts/validation/test_enumerate_decision_mutants_r2_regression.sh:94`, a mutant replacement string) got the audited `# killpg-gate:allow` marker. Built on vehicle `vehicle/rel734` from `main` at `fc57ad0c`, after the independent review of the first v73.4 release commit (`e3a55a44`, NO-GO: I-1 to I-4).

#### What changed

- **§11.4.263 (review I-1).** `bash scripts/gates/cm_killpg_pgid_guard.sh` on `fc57ad0c` printed FAIL with 7 unguarded process-group signal sites (27 guarded). Each is now guarded by an `int > 1` check at the call, or (a saved function reference, an error message the gate read as a call, a shell line inside a Python string template) marked with the audited `# killpg-gate:allow <reason>` in a real comment: `scripts/fastcycle/tests/test_baseline_replay_r2_findings.sh`, `scripts/fastcycle/tests/test_fc_supervisor_v4r5_findings.sh` (rendered shim byte-identical apart from the added `-gt 1` test), `scripts/full_suite/run_full_suite.py`, `tests/test_run_full_suite.sh`, `scripts/validation/test_meta_test_verification_r1_regression.sh` (2), `scripts/validation/enumerate_decision_mutants.py`. Gate after: PASS, 31 sites guarded, 13 allowed by marker. Reverting each file alone makes the gate FAIL again (6 of 6). A separate `git grep` sweep with positive and negative control needles found no further live site.
- **Version badge.** `scripts/badges/compute_badges.sh` reads `PROVISIONAL` in the first CHANGELOG heading in any letter case; `docs/BADGES.md` says so. Selftest gained the lowercase case.
- **Release cut (review I-2, I-3).** New `scripts/release/derive_release_facts.sh` (every count and id list of a release entry from committed data and the tracker, including the §11.4.290(4) count line), `scripts/release/render_release_entry.py` (fills a template, refuses any unfilled key), `scripts/release/tag_and_release.sh` (preflight of every remote before any write, `git push --atomic` per remote, read-back gate, releases only for remotes read back OK, resume, never force, non-zero on any failure), the v73.4 entry template and `docs/release/README.md`. Tests: `tests/release/test_tag_and_release.sh` (45 checks, 9 mutants killed), `tests/release/test_derive_release_facts.sh` (29 checks, 9 mutants killed; no value read from the tracker is put back into SQL text, so a severity or state containing a quote neither breaks nor alters a query, and the previous version fails that case), `tests/release/test_render_release_entry.sh` (16 checks, 5 mutants killed). The old reviewed script, run in the same harness, exited 0 while one remote kept its old `main` and got the tag.

#### Honest boundaries

- `gh` and `glab` are stubs in the release tests (unit class, §11.4.289(C)); no real platform release has been made with the script.
- The two vehicle commits carry no `Roadmap-Item:` trailer and no item was registered before the work (review m-8, §11.4.293(A)); they were not rewritten (§11.4.113). The item CON-226 was registered at landing and the landing merge commit carries `Roadmap-Item: CON-226`.

## Unreleased — 2026-11-07 — ATM-1809 follow-ups of audit 2 of §11.4.297 / §11.4.298 (head `4076904d`, verdict GO-with-follow-ups): text pins in both anchor-wired gates, the account-name gate's look-alike, filler, NUL and empty-list gaps closed, and four wording fixes (the `generated-regeneration` reading stated as an interpretation, the two admitted waits, one open pull request per task id at a time, the reviewer refusal and the kept submodule branch)

**Status of this entry: UNRELEASED; implemented on host evidence only; not VERIFIED-DONE; not accepted.** No tag exists for it. A follow-up series on top of `4076904d` (the head of the §11.4.297 / §11.4.298 pull request), prepared in a scratch clone with no remote (nothing was pushed from it), then applied with `git am --3way` on upstream `main` `d374d535` (the merge of PR 5, whose tree is the tree of `4076904d`: every applied commit has the tree of its scratch commit, the tip tree is `ab48aa1f`, no conflict) on branch `task/ATM-1809-pr-delivery-audit2-followups`, and delivered by the pull request of that branch. Its five content commits are the stable keys by subject: the account-name gate (F7), the `generated-regeneration` interpretation (F10), the two admitted waits (F11), the wording residuals (F12), the text pins (F1); this entry is the sixth. Later commits fix the blocking findings of audit 3 (`e3a7e6b7` B1; `a9784ca7` and `09e5c2a2` B2) and of its re-audit (`adbdd013` R1, with `78bd4adb` for its minor finding BW1), and two bring this entry up to date (`4738d01d` and the commit that carries this sentence). Driver: independent audit 2 of `4076904d` (2026-11-07), verdict GO-with-follow-ups; its minor-class findings in the head are F1, F7, F10, F11 and F12 (its F2 to F6, F8 and F9 lie outside this repository, F13 in the pull-request body). **No independent review of this series had run when this entry was first written. An independent audit of the applied series (audit 3, head `e0046ef6`, 2026-11-07 UTC) returned NO-GO with two blocking findings, B1 and B2, fixed by `e3a7e6b7`, `a9784ca7` and `09e5c2a2` (see "Audit 3" below). A re-audit of `4738d01d` returned NO-GO with one blocking finding, R1, fixed by `adbdd013`. The head that is merged is audited again before the merge; that GO and its findings are recorded in the delivering pull request, not here.**

#### Corrections to the entry of the §11.4.297 / §11.4.298 pull request below (that entry is not rewritten; its stale status sentence carries a pointer here)

- Its status line says "nothing was pushed" and "The fixed tip has not been audited again"; both were true when written. PR 5 was pushed and merged as `d374d535` (a merge commit, 2026-11-07T22:14:13Z; one review on the platform, a comment review of head `4076904d` with 8 line comments from the author agent's account, which also merged it), and audit 2 of that tip ran on 2026-11-07 and returned GO-with-follow-ups for head `4076904d`; this entry lists the follow-ups.
- Its honest boundaries and (H)(2) said the anchor-wired gates prove the rule "complete": they proved the marker phrases present, and audit 2 kept every marker while weakening the text in 27 of 30 ways. The text now says what the gates prove (the phrases and the pinned wording).

#### What changed

- **F1, text pins.** `cm_pr_delivery_anchor_wired.sh` pins, by the sha256 of the text with all whitespace removed, the §11.4.297 canon block, the §11.4.297 block of each mirror and the 2026-11-07 refinement notes of §11.4.288, §11.4.292 and §11.4.293; `cm_release_gate_anchor_wired.sh` pins the §11.4.298 canon block, its mirror block and the release-gate note of §11.4.293. Each note is pinned as its whole paragraph since `e3a7e6b7`; before it, only the marker line of the note was pinned and a continuation line passed (audit 3, B1); until `adbdd013` a line that starts with `#` but is no heading ("#1 ...", seven `#`) still ended the paragraph (re-audit, R1). Any change other than whitespace fails until the pin is updated in the gate, a reviewed edit in the same commit (`--print-digests` prints the current values); a digest control needle makes a digest that keeps whitespace or ignores a changed word BLIND. The honest boundaries of §11.4.297 and §11.4.298 and (H)(2) say a pin proves the wording is the reviewed one, never that it is right. Both mutation tests run each of audit 2's marker-preserving weakenings (P1 to P20, R1 to R10) as a RED case named by its pin, a whitespace-only edit in a pinned mirror block and note as golden-FALSE, `--print-digests` against the pins, a re-pinned gate copy, a gate copy with the pin check disabled (the change then passes) and the broken digest.
- **F7, the account-name gate.** The confusables table gains the Cyrillic and Greek letters audit 2 found missed (U+043A, U+043C, U+0442, U+043F, U+0433, U+0475, U+0461, U+04BD, U+0437, U+0431, U+04C0, U+0417, U+03F2, U+03F3, U+03B3, U+03C7) and is applied before NFKD as well as after it; the Hangul and Braille fillers (U+115F, U+1160, U+3164, U+FFA0, U+2800) are dropped; a skeleton scan reads, inside a word that holds a letter, a 1, a bar, an I or a Cyrillic or Greek look-alike of i or l as either letter and a 0 as o (and a long s for f; since `a9784ca7` the digits and bars of a number, a date, a serial or a shell `||` are not read as letters: audit 3, B2, found every section number read as two letters l); a corpus file with a NUL byte and a present but empty reviewer list are BLIND. The honest boundary names what is still not undone.
- **F10.** §11.4.297(E)(3) and the §11.4.293 note state that filing a tool-written ledger row under `generated-regeneration` is an INTERPRETATION of §11.4.293(A)(4), not an amendment, covering only rows that tooling writes to a listed path.
- **F11.** The §11.4.288 note and point (A.2) state the two waits that the merge-as-landing admits against the "no approval wait" of §11.4.288(B)(1): an approval the project configures as required and, under a human merge gate, the human merger.
- **F12.** One open pull request per task id at a time ((B)(2), the heading, the mirror, the README summary); (C)(4) never uses a delete-at-merge option for a submodule pull request ((F)(2) keeps that branch until the parent lands); (D)(4): a reported refusal never closes the pull request and meets merge condition (c); the mirror headline names the source-correctness GO and the mirror (C) summary names the configured approvals.

#### Audit 3 (independent, 2026-11-07 UTC, head `e0046ef6`): NO-GO, the two blocking findings fixed

- **B1, fixed by `e3a7e6b7`.** Both anchor-wired gates read a refinement note with `grep -F` of its marker line, so a sentence on the next line (the same Markdown paragraph, hence part of the note) passed every gate; reproduced on `e0046ef6`: a line "A squash merge is also a landing under pull-request delivery." under the §11.4.288 note kept `PASS — 305/305`. The gates now read the note as its whole paragraph (`note_para`, with its own control needle); the pinned digests are unchanged because every note is a one-line paragraph. After the fix the same line under each of the four notes fails its pin, and a note re-wrapped across lines (a whitespace-only change) passes. Mutation tests on `e3a7e6b7`: `PASS: 361 RED/BLIND cases and 10 GREEN cases (1 control + 9 golden-FALSE)` and `PASS: 200 RED/BLIND cases and 10 GREEN cases (1 control + 9 golden-FALSE)` (356 + 9 and 197 + 9 before).
- **B2, fixed by `a9784ca7` and `09e5c2a2`.** The skeleton form mapped every 0, 1 and bar, so numbers read as letters; reproduced on `e0046ef6`: an account of the two letters l and i given by `--forbid-login` gave exit 1 with 18844 FAIL lines (0 at `d374d535`). The mappings now apply only inside a word that holds a letter. FAIL lines over the 366 corpus files for an account given by `--forbid-login`, `d374d535` / `e0046ef6` / after the fix: the letters l, o, l: 0 / 359 / 0; l, o, o: 0 / 183 / 0; o, i, l: 0 / 1 / 0; l, i: 0 / 18844 / 296 (no account name is written here: the gate reads this file). Audit 2's disguise sweep gives a byte-identical result before and after the fix (no disguise lost). The first run of the extended mutation test on `a9784ca7` failed (two golden-FALSE cases used accounts the gate source holds, and two bar mutants survived because the new header text carried a disguised fixture account); `09e5c2a2` fixes both. Mutation test on `09e5c2a2`: `PASS: 79 cases behaved as required and 38 of 38 gate mutants were killed` without the consumer, `PASS: 82 cases behaved as required and 38 of 38 gate mutants were killed` with it.
- **R1 (re-audit of `4738d01d`, NO-GO), fixed by `adbdd013`.** `note_para` ended a note at every line that starts with `#`; in CommonMark and GFM such a line is a heading only when one to six `#` (after at most three spaces) are followed by a blank or the end of the line, so "#1 ..." or a line of seven `#` under or above a note is part of it and escaped the pin. Reproduced on `4738d01d`: a "#1" line under the §11.4.288 note kept `PASS — 305/305`; with the fix it fails naming `PIN_NOTE_288`. The note now ends only at a blank line or a real ATX heading, and its control needle holds both kinds of `#` line. Mutation tests on `78bd4adb`: `PASS: 365 RED/BLIND cases and 10 GREEN cases (1 control + 9 golden-FALSE)` and `PASS: 202 RED/BLIND cases and 10 GREEN cases (1 control + 9 golden-FALSE)`.
- **BW1 (minor, re-audit), covered by `78bd4adb`.** No case covered the hyphen of the skeleton word class; a RED case (a hyphenated account whose last part is written with digits) and a gate mutant without the hyphen now do. Account-name mutation test on `78bd4adb`: `PASS: 80 cases behaved as required and 39 of 39 gate mutants were killed` without the consumer, `PASS: 83 cases behaved as required and 39 of 39 gate mutants were killed` with it.
- **Not fixed here, open follow-ups of audit 3** (minor and nit, recorded for a later change): the pin digest removes all whitespace, so splitting or joining a word keeps a pin (M1; the canon says "with all whitespace removed"); a disguise glued to a table bar and the letters U+0269, U+1D0F and U+0585 are still missed by the skeleton (M2); the mutation tests do not discriminate the U+01C0 mapping, the i side of the i/l class, the upper-case Greek row, the U+2212 minus or a weakened §11.4.292 note pin in a gate copy (M3; the gate header no longer claims every step has a mutant); `--changed-since` is BLIND when the change set holds a binary file (M4); the F10 sentence "and any other path, is never `generated-regeneration`" reads, literally, wider than §11.4.293(A)(4) (M5); two sentences of (C)(4) read across repositories can wait on each other (M6); (H)(2) still says the normaliser "undoes look-alike letters of other scripts" (M7, text older than this series); the §11.4.298 boundary says "the refinement notes it adds" for one note (N1); text outside the pinned blocks, a later note included, is not pinned (N2); the account-name gate takes about twice as long as at `d374d535` (N3); a separate pin-bump pull request under (F)(3) and "one open pull request per task id at a time" under (B)(2) read together are ambiguous (N4).

#### PROVISIONAL / UNCONFIRMED

- No §11.4.142 / §11.4.209 review of this series existed when this entry was first written; audit 3 is an independent audit by another agent session, not a human review, and no human has accepted this change.
- Whether the operator prefers the F10 reading as an interpretation or as a stated amendment point of §11.4.293 is UNCONFIRMED; so is the operator's view of "at a time" in (B)(2).
- A pin is only as good as the review of the edit that updates it: an author who changes the text and the pin together passes the gate; the review of that commit is the control.
- The account-name gate still does not undo letters of other scripts (Armenian, Cherokee, Canadian syllabics, Lisu and others), Latin small capitals and other Latin look-alikes, symbols such as APL, bidirectional reordering, character references, percent-encoding or markup that splits a name; audit 2's sweep has 67 such confusable variants, 5 bidi, 10 encoding and 10 markup variants still MISSED, all outside the stated boundary.
- Host evidence only. The delivering pull request, its self-review, its CI flag and its merge are recorded on the platform and in the consuming project's evidence, not in this entry; the merge read-back and the pushes to the mirrors happen after this text is fixed and are not claimed here.
- The skeleton still over-reports short accounts that hold an i or an l, because i and l match each other: FAIL lines over the corpus, `d374d535` / now, for an account of the letters l and i: 0 / 296 (roman numerals, labels of a letter and a digit, contractions); l and o: 2 / 47; i and o: 42 / 48. This is the safe direction stated in the gate's honest boundary; a consumer with such an account would see a red gate.

## Unreleased — 2026-11-07 — §11.4.297 pull-request delivery (a declared mode: one task branch and one pull request per task id, configured reviewers requested and read back and never named in the constitution, a self-review and merge by the author agent unless a human merge gate is configured, landing only by fast-forward or merge commit, read back and retired, evidence resolvable from the head tree, submodule pull request before the pin bump with mirror parity read back, enforcement words) with the explicit reconciliation with §11.4.288; and §11.4.298 release gate per product (a full LIVE run of every existing test on connected target hardware of the product before any deployment or tag, a closed four-value gate state)

**Status of this entry: UNRELEASED; implemented on host evidence only; not VERIFIED-DONE; not accepted.** No tag exists for it. Prepared in a scratch clone (shared object store, every push URL set to `dead://`) on branch `task/ATM-1809-pr-delivery-rules` from upstream `main` `6dd9eca4` (merge of PR 3); two content commits (`c87745de` §11.4.297, `2d783401` §11.4.298), the entry (`3220f22d`), two commits that fix the findings of an independent audit (`c0ab2055` canon and wired gates, `78269020` account-name gate), the merge of the moved upstream `main` `e5fed962` (PR 4; `50c602fe`) and this update of the entry; nothing was pushed. The commit ids are those of the scratch branch and change if the series is applied elsewhere; the subjects are the stable keys. **An independent audit of `3220f22d` returned NO-GO; its findings and their fixes are listed under "Audit fixes" below. The fixed tip has not been audited again.** (Pointer added by the follow-up entry above: that sentence and "nothing was pushed" are corrected there; audit 2 of `4076904d` ran on 2026-11-07, verdict GO-with-follow-ups.) Drivers, each relayed by the conductor of a consuming project on 2026-11-07, identifiers UNCONFIRMED: (1) an operator order: every pull-request rule that project had adopted at project level is carried into the root constitution, fully decoupled and reusable, except the account names of assignees and reviewers; (2) an operator rule: the agent that creates a pull request self-reviews it (real review comments in the pull request) and then merges it, the reviewer account names staying project configuration; (3) an operator note (17:13): an image or build is deployed or tagged only after full deterministic LIVE testing on connected target hardware and a proven no-worse comparison, per product, a product without hardware or baseline being BLOCKED. The consuming project's recorded conflict between its pull-request delivery decision and §11.4.288 (its precedence register calls it C-19) is the conflict §11.4.297(A) resolves.

#### What changed

- **Anchor §11.4.297** (`Constitution.md`; the next free number: the highest heading at `6dd9eca4` is §11.4.296, and no branch of the upstream holds a §11.4.297: the six `wip/continue-*` heads were fetched and read; the next free deferral id was `OWED-GATE-182`): clauses (A) declaration (merger `author-agent` by default, `human` = a human merge gate) and the reconciliation with §11.4.288, (B) one task, one branch, one pull request (plain `Roadmap-Item:` value, `Multi-Task-Reason:`, no foreign commit, push read back with `git ls-remote`, serial landing of pull requests that touch a declared shared single file, the commit and publish steps never merge), (C) self-review and merge by the author agent unless a human merge gate is configured, read back, then retired, (D) reviewers: a configured list (default key `pr_reviewers`), the author skip, request and read-back, a refused reviewer reported with the platform's text and never dropped, no account named here, (E) evidence a reviewer can resolve (repository-relative path and sha256 in the head tree, else INCONCLUSIVE) and tool-written ledger rows under `generated-regeneration`, (F) submodule pull requests, the pin bump after the merge read-back and the mirror-parity table `PRESENT` / `MIRROR-LAG <tip>` / `UNREACHABLE`, (G) the enforcement words `ENFORCED` / `PROVISIONAL` / `UNCONFIRMED`, (H) gates, tests and paired mutations, (I) what the anchor does not change.
- **The self-review and the merge, clause (C).** The author agent reads the whole diff of the head it will merge and posts a review on the pull request that names that head, what it checked and the source-correctness GO it relies on; every finding is a line comment, answered by a follow-up commit (whose delta is reviewed again) or a written reply. An approval alone, a bare "looks good" or an empty body is not a self-review; a platform that refuses the author's approval gets a comment review, never a review through another account. It merges only when the GO (recorded for exactly the head being merged, as a record the merger reads), its read-back self-review of that head, the reviewer read-back, an unmoved head (pinned in the merge call where the platform offers it), the serial order, any approval the project configures as required and the remote base tip being an ancestor of the reviewed head (fetched right before the merge; a moved base is merged into the task branch and the new head reviewed again) are read back, and only in the declared landing form; a platform option that deletes the branch at merge time is used only when the platform is the only declared upstream. Under a human merge gate (a recorded decision) no agent merges. INTERPRETATION written in the clause (UNCONFIRMED by the operator): the self-review is added and never replaces the independent source-correctness GO of §11.4.288 / §11.4.142 / §11.4.235 (§11.4.240: the producer never gates its own change); a reading in which the self-review is the GO needs its own operator decision.
- **The reconciliation with §11.4.288, stated, not silent.** A project that declares pull-request delivery by a recorded operator decision gets exactly three amendments of §11.4.288 and no other: (A.1) the decision is the recorded vehicle necessity of (B)(2); (A.2) the merge of a pull request that holds its source-correctness GO, by its author agent after the read-back self-review or by the human merger under a human merge gate, is the landing step of (B)(1), and a pull request still open after the declared merge window is reported as stale; (A.3) the landing is a fast-forward or a merge commit whose second parent is the reviewed head; squash and rebase merges are forbidden because they leave the reviewed commits off `main` (the (B)(4) count never reaches zero and a parent gitlink to a submodule commit is not on that submodule's `main`). Every other sentence of §11.4.288 binds such a project unchanged ((B)(4) retirement, (D) submodule first, fast-forward pushes of `main`, no force); a project without the declaration keeps §11.4.288 as written. The amendment is an operator decision: this entry proposes it and does not decide it.
- **Anchor §11.4.298** (the next free number after §11.4.297; deferral `OWED-GATE-185`). It restates nothing that already holds (§11.4.40 full retest before a tag, §11.4.135 regression guard, §11.4.200 verify after write, §11.4.236 deploy readiness, §11.4.289 live only, §11.4.290(G) release labelling, §11.4.293(B) to (F) the comparison and its three verdicts) and adds exactly four things: (A) the scope is every deployable build (flashing image, update package, application package; kinds are consumer DATA), every deployment (any act that puts it anywhere but its own test hardware) and every tag; (B) the live run re-runs every existing test of the product's suite, deterministic and LIVE, on physical hardware of that product connected while the run executes and identified from the target, never an emulator, a host run, a replayed record or another product's hardware; (C) the gate holds per product, one product's verdict never standing for another, a multi-product release needing every covered product `RELEASABLE`; (D) a closed gate state `RELEASABLE` / `BLOCKED-NO-HARDWARE` / `BLOCKED-NO-BASELINE` / `REFUSED-REGRESSED`, only `RELEASABLE` green, a product without a live-run record `BLOCKED-NO-HARDWARE`. Clause (E) amends §11.4.293(F) at exactly one point (no tag of any class for a build without a completed live run on its product's connected hardware) and keeps the labelled dev-class tag of a `BLOCKED-NO-BASELINE` or `REFUSED-REGRESSED` build, which is never deployed; INTERPRETATION written in the clause (UNCONFIRMED): the note's "tagged" is read as every tag for the live-run condition and as a release tag for the no-worse condition. (F) one machine-written gate record per product. (G) what is not changed. The text names no product and no device.

- **How the account-name gate keeps names out**: it reads the consumer's reviewer and assignee accounts at run time from the consumer's own delivery configuration (`<root>/../config/delivery.conf` when the constitution is a submodule, or `--consumer-config`, `--reviewers-key`, `--assignees-key`, `--forbid-login`) and scans its corpus for each as a whole name, case-insensitively, in the raw text and in a normalised form (compatibility decomposition, format characters and combining marks dropped, a Cyrillic and Greek look-alike table, hyphen look-alikes mapped to `-`). The corpus is the rule files (canon, four carriers, README, CHANGELOG, index, groups, progressive rule files, catalog and rules manifest, the owed-gates page) and every file class this change added or changed (the pull-request and release-gate gates and their tests, the generated propagation gates, the three registries, the hub test, the post-update hook, the suite manifest, the anchor library and its test, every guide); `--changed-since REV` adds every file changed since REV. A `PASS` says nothing about a file outside the corpus; neither the gate nor its mutation test stores an account name. It answers `PASS` only when at least one account was read, `SKIP` (exit 3) on a constitution checked out alone, `BLIND` (exit 2) when a configuration exists and cannot be read. The anchor-wired gate reads the account-name gate's token from that gate's own `GATE=` line, so the §11.4.227(A) ledger attributes each new gate to its own file.
- **Not in this change**: the delivery tool, the merge step, the hosting-platform client, the comparator, the deployment and tag tooling and the four item-level gates (deferrals): the constitution ships the rules, the consuming project implements the seams (§11.4.6, §11.4.227). No status set and no verdict set of another anchor is extended; §11.4.293's three verdicts are unchanged and §11.4.298's states are computed from them.

## Unreleased — 2026-11-07 — ATM-1809 hardening of the work-to-track guard test after the review of PR 3: TMPDIR is resolved to an existing physical directory (relative, missing and unusable values handled), the hook's track derivation from the cwd is covered, and every refusal of the suite names its own cause

**Status of this entry: UNRELEASED; implemented on host evidence only; not VERIFIED-DONE; not accepted.** No tag exists for it. Prepared in a scratch clone (every push URL set to `dead://`) on branch `task/ATM-1809-guard-test-hardening-2` from upstream `main` `6dd9eca4` (the merge of PR 3; `gh pr view 3`: MERGED 2026-11-07T10:53:09Z by the operator's account, 0 reviews, no review request); one code commit plus this entry; the worker pushed nothing. The commit ids are those of the scratch branch and change if the series is applied elsewhere; the subject is the stable key. **No independent review of this change has run.** Driver: the independent read-only review of PR 3 (`/mnt/track4/t1_review_prs_20261107/REVIEW.md`, findings F1 to F4; its F5 is the first correction below).

#### Corrections to the entry of PR 3 below (that entry is not rewritten)

- "nothing was pushed" and "No independent review of this change has run" were true when written. PR 3 was pushed and merged as `6dd9eca4` (2026-11-07T10:53:09Z, 0 GitHub reviews) and a read-only independent review of it exists: GO on the code, with F1 to be corrected.
- "this change makes the suite independent of it [TMPDIR]" was **false** (F1). With a relative TMPDIR and the cwd outside a track mount the suite still failed 8 cases (37 PASS, 8 FAIL: `TMP` was never made absolute, so `WI_DB=rel/...` stopped resolving after `cd "$REPO"`). With a TMPDIR naming no directory the first `mktemp -d` was unchecked, `TMP` was empty, `cd ""` succeeded and the fixtures were built at `/registry.db` and `/repo`; the run then ended in a misleading `track '<none read>'` (from inside a track mount it relocated and passed, so the outcome depended on the cwd). The claim holds only as shown by the matrix in the VERIFIED list below.
- The mutant list M1 to M5 of that entry did not say that no case ran the hook from a cwd under `/mnt/track<N>` (F2): the hook's cwd-to-track derivation and the `--track "$T_CUR"` it hands the resolver were exercised by no case, and since PR 3 every commit-path case runs at track `?` on purpose. Three hook mutants (track always `?`, `--track '?'` passed to the resolver, logical `pwd`) passed all 45 cases, on the file of PR 3 and on the file before it.

#### What changed

- `scripts/hooks/test_guard_work_track_binding.sh` (the only code file; +230/-28 lines, 52 of the added lines are comments). The hook, the resolver and the 45 earlier cases are unchanged.
- F1, TMPDIR: the fixture root is built under `${TMPDIR:-/tmp}` after the value is resolved with `cd -- ... && pwd -P` before anything changes directory (so a relative value, a value with a leading `-` and a symlink all work), the `mktemp` result is checked, and the root is moved to `/tmp` when the physical path is under `/mnt/track<N>`. A TMPDIR that is not a directory (missing, a file, a dangling link), is not writable, or whose `/tmp` fallback is unusable ends in `PRECONDITION FAIL: <one cause>` and exit 1. An unusable TMPDIR is never silently replaced by `/tmp`. The `EXIT` trap removes the root by its absolute path (a relative path starting with `-` was read by `rm` as options and left the directory behind).
- F2, track derivation: new section E, 11 cases. 4 run at a cwd outside any track mount (two resolver-direct controls prove the two pins bite; a file pinned to the layout's track and one pinned to another are both allowed at track `?`). 7 run at a cwd whose PHYSICAL path is `/mnt/track<N>/...`: a layout control, this-track file allowed, other-track file BLOCKED, the refusal names `track 'track-<N>'`, the resolver text inside the refusal carries `but the change is on track 'track-<N>'`, a cwd reached through a symlink that lives outside the mount (physical = track-<N>) BLOCKED, and a cwd reached through a symlink that lives inside the mount and points outside (physical = outside) allowed. The oracle is the hook's own exit code and refusal text. Every ALLOW case checks that its file really got staged (99 otherwise), so an empty index cannot pass. The registry gains two probe groups (`trk-here`, `trk-away`) pinned to track-N and track-N+1.
- The layout (`GWTB_TRACK_LAYOUT=auto|ns|real|none`, default `auto`): a private user and mount namespace made with unprivileged `unshare -Urm` (tmpfs on `/mnt`, every entry of the real `/mnt` bound back in, plus `/mnt/track12`, two digits on purpose; nothing outside the namespace is touched; the same preparation script is used for the availability probe and for the run), else a writable `/mnt/track<N>` of the host (a `gwtb_track.*` directory, removed on exit), else the 7 cases are printed as `SKIP`, counted apart and announced in a `skipped:` line (`RESULT: PASS (49 cases run, 7 skipped)`); never counted as PASS. `ns` and `real` refuse with a `PRECONDITION FAIL` when that source is unavailable; `none` skips on purpose. The child's PASS/FAIL lines are counted and a child that reports fewer than its 7 cases is itself a FAIL.
- F3, diagnosis: the control needle ends in its own `PRECONDITION FAIL` per cause: probe file not staged, hook allowed the known BLOCK (fail-open), hook exited other than 2 (with its first stderr line), refusal without the `Current checkout` line, a line of another shape, derived track other than `?`. Before, all of them printed `track '<none read>'`.
- F4: the unused `EMPTYDIR` is removed.
- Not changed: the hook, `multitrack_work_binding.sh`, any anchor, `Constitution.md`, the carriers, the generated files, `config/full_suite_manifest.tsv` (the test has its row: `sh entry default bash - auto`), `docs/scripts/guard-work-track-binding.md`.

- On a host with neither an unprivileged user and mount namespace nor a writable `/mnt/track<N>` the 7 inside-layout cases are SKIPPED and the exit code is 0; H1, H2, H5, H6 and H9 are not caught there. This is announced on the screen and recorded by the runner, not hidden, but it is a gap of that host class. With a host track mount of one digit only, H6 (two-digit track) is not caught either; the namespace layout uses `track12`.
- The namespace layout runs the hook as uid 0 of a user namespace (the real user is mapped to it). The hook's `git` calls and the registry reads behaved as with the real user (the same 56 cases pass under the host layout), but a behaviour that depends on a real uid is not exercised.
- Stale text seen and left alone: `docs/scripts/guard-work-track-binding.md` still says "34 cases" (the suite has 56 now) and does not mention section E or `GWTB_TRACK_LAYOUT`; `progressive/EVIDENCE/PREFLIGHT_MANIFEST.json` holds a sha256 for this test that differs from the file; no script of this repository reads that entry and it was not touched.
- The consumer's launcher still exports a TMPDIR under `/mnt/track<N>`; the suite no longer depends on it, the launcher is out of this repository.


## Unreleased — 2026-11-07 — ATM-1809 hermetic test of the work-to-track guard: `test_guard_work_track_binding.sh` no longer fails two ALLOW cases when TMPDIR sits under `/mnt/track<N>`, and refuses to run when its fixture premise is false

**Status of this entry: UNRELEASED; implemented on host evidence only; not VERIFIED-DONE; not accepted.** No tag exists for it. Prepared in a scratch clone (shared object store, every push URL set to `dead://`) on branch `task/ATM-1809-guard-test-tmpdir` from upstream `main` `85281b59` (merge of PR 2); one code commit (`b4de1298`) plus this entry; nothing was pushed. The commit ids are those of the scratch branch and change if the series is applied elsewhere; the subject is the stable key. **No independent review of this change has run.** Driver: the consuming repository's pre-build run r11 (2026-11-06) exported a TMPDIR under `/mnt/track4/` and the suite below reported 43 PASS, 2 FAIL while the hook behaved as designed.

#### What changed

- `scripts/hooks/test_guard_work_track_binding.sh` (the only file changed, +31 lines). Cause (captured): `guard-work-track-binding.sh` `current_track` derives the track from the PHYSICAL cwd (`/mnt/track<N>/...` gives `track-<N>`, anything else `?`), and every commit-path case of the suite runs the hook from a cwd inside a `mktemp -d` fixture and is written for track `?`. With TMPDIR under `/mnt/track4` the fixture sat under a track mount, the hook derived `track-4`, and the two pinned-track ALLOW cases (`mistiq file staged on feature/mistiq-vader allowed`, `single-track: unified file staged on main allowed`) were BLOCKED. The hook's refusal text names `track 'track-4'`; the test, not the hook, was wrong.
- Fix: (1) the fixture root is moved to `/tmp` (`mktemp -d /tmp/gwtb.XXXXXX`) when the TMPDIR-based root resolves under `/mnt/track<N>`; when no root outside a track mount can be made the suite prints `PRECONDITION FAIL` and exits 1 instead of running cases whose premise is false. (2) A control needle reads the track the hook itself derives for the fixture cwd (a mistiq file staged on `main` is a known BLOCK whose refusal names the track) and refuses with `PRECONDITION FAIL` unless it is `?`; a needle that reads nothing refuses as well. The hook, the resolver and every one of the 45 cases are unchanged.
- Not changed: the hook, `multitrack_work_binding.sh`, any anchor, `Constitution.md`, the carriers, the generated files, `config/full_suite_manifest.tsv` (the test already has its row: `sh entry default bash - auto`), `docs/scripts/guard-work-track-binding.md`.

#### VERIFIED (commands run on 2026-11-07 in the scratch clone, output seen; the script is `verify.sh` in the scratch directory, outputs under `ev/`)

- RED before, GREEN after (the real test on the real hook, started from the cwd `/mnt/track1/atmosphere-t1` (the suite changes into its own fixture), one TMPDIR per run): the file at base `85281b59` gives 45 PASS, 0 FAIL with TMPDIR outside `/mnt/track<N>` (control), 43 PASS, 2 FAIL with TMPDIR under `/mnt/track4/...` and 43 PASS, 2 FAIL with the exact directory the consumer's run used (`/mnt/track4/t1_prebuild_r11_20261106/tmp`), the two failing cases being the two named above. The fixed file gives 45 PASS, 0 FAIL in all three settings and with TMPDIR unset; in the two track-mount runs the fixture root was `/tmp/gwtb.*` and the precondition line read `the hook derives track '?' for the fixture cwd`. No `/tmp/gwtb.*` and no fixture directory is left behind.
- Paired mutants of the fixed file (each applied to a copy, the real tree untouched): M1 relocation removed (needle kept): `PRECONDITION FAIL ... track 'track-4'`, exit 1 under a track-mount TMPDIR, 45 PASS under an outside TMPDIR; M2 needle made blind (pattern no longer matches the refusal text): `PRECONDITION FAIL ... track '<none read>'`, exit 1; M3 the `/tmp` fallback itself placed under a track mount: `PRECONDITION FAIL: no fixture root outside /mnt/track<N>`, exit 1; M4 needle and relocation both off: the original 2 FAIL return (43 PASS, 2 FAIL) under a track-mount TMPDIR; M5 the superproject walk disabled in the hook and the resolver (the existing pre-build paired mutant): 43 PASS, 2 FAIL (`submodule: unclassified file on main allowed (parent DB found)`, `submodule: sub-work file on feature/sub-work allowed`) in both TMPDIR settings, so the new precondition does not mask a real regression of the hook.
- Static: `bash -n` clean; whole-file `shellcheck --norc -s bash -e SC1090,SC1091` 0 errors, 0 warnings before and after; info-level notes only: SC2016 on two existing lines (before and after) and one new SC2015 (info) on the `A && B || C` of the fallback, which is the intended form (`||` runs the refusal when either step fails).
- Gates of this repository over the change: `bash scripts/run_full_suite.sh --verify-manifest` `MANIFEST-OK`, rc 0 (census total 861, unchanged); `bash scripts/badges/compute_badges.sh --check` rc 0; the full-suite runner on the one unit (`--only test_guard_work_track_binding --allow-topology-gaps`, TMPDIR under `/mnt/track4`, scratch clone with uninitialised submodules, so the overall RESULT is `PARTIAL-TOPOLOGY`, exit 8, not a full retest): the unit `PASS`, rc 0, 45 cases; the review pre-check (`precheck_pack_build.sh --commit 85281b59..b4de1298`) `all_pass: true`, 10 of 10 checks PASS (parse, shellcheck, affected-gates, touched-gate-mutations, secret-scan, doc-sync, closure-evidence-class, sibling-search, blast-radius, already-fixed-markers).

#### PROVISIONAL / UNCONFIRMED

- Independent §11.4.142 / §11.4.209 review of this change: none has run.
- Host evidence only: the on-device and consumer-pre-build effect is not verified here. UNCONFIRMED: that the consumer's next pre-build run reads the row green (the row's verdict depends on its own pinned pattern; this change was proven on the test, not on the row).
- The pre-check pack's `affected-gates` and `touched-gate-mutations` are the constants recorded in the previous entry's finding 7, so those two PASS lines prove nothing about the diff; the mutants above were run by hand.
- Fail-closed by design: a future hook that derives the track some other way than from the physical cwd would make the needle refuse until the test is adapted.
- Stale text seen and left alone: `docs/scripts/guard-work-track-binding.md` still says "34 cases" (the suite has 45), and `progressive/EVIDENCE/PREFLIGHT_MANIFEST.json` holds a sha256 for this test (`f04e0e85...`) that already differed from the file at base (`210014a2...`); no script in this repository reads that manifest entry, and it was not touched.
- The consumer's launcher still exports a TMPDIR under `/mnt/track<N>`; this change makes the suite independent of it and does not alter the launcher (out of this repository).

## Unreleased — 2026-11-06 — ATM-1809 follow-ups of the two gate fixes (review round 2, non-blocking): CM-KILLPG-PGID-GUARD no longer ends in SKIP for a symlinked or prune-named root, lists only real exclusions, and has its would-scan gap covered; CM-MULTITRACK-ENGINE-IN-CONSTITUTION reads env's split-string forms and prints a python error as one line; the two SQLite sidecars that an Auto-commit put in the tree are removed and ignored

**Status of this entry: UNRELEASED; implemented on host evidence only; not VERIFIED-DONE; not accepted.** No tag exists for it. Prepared in a scratch clone (shared object store, every push URL set to `dead://`) on branch `task/ATM-1809-gate-followups` from upstream `main` `44a04d43`; one commit per finding; nothing was pushed. The findings are the non-blocking follow-ups of independent review round 2 of the gate fixes landed by PR 1 (merge `a462a257`), as relayed by the conductor; the review text is not in this repository (UNCONFIRMED: its exact wording and verdict). The commit ids below are those of the scratch branch and change when the series is applied elsewhere; the subjects are the stable key. No independent review of this change has run.

#### What changed

- Sidecars (`ce1fde17`, the conductor's added finding): `git rm docs/workable_items.db-shm docs/workable_items.db-wal` (committed by `844ab543`, "Auto-commit 1793970817873", a 32768-byte -shm and an empty -wal), both added to the root `.gitignore`, new `tests/gates/test_workable_items_db_sidecars_ignored.sh` plus its row in `config/full_suite_manifest.tsv` and the synced census line of `docs/guides/full_suite_runner_guide.md` (the full-suite runner refuses, exit 3, a census file without a row and a stale guide census) (S1 the database stays tracked and unignored, S2 both sidecars ignored, S3 none tracked, S4 a scratch repository with this `.gitignore` and a copy of the database, a real sqlite3 connection held open, `git add -A` stages the unrelated file and no sidecar, M1 the same without the two entries stages both, M2 a force-added sidecar is reported). Root cause is under PROVISIONAL / UNCONFIRMED below.
- M1 (`84af129d`): `scripts/gates/cm_killpg_pgid_guard.sh` resolves a directory `--root` with `pwd -P` and prints a NOTE when the given path is a symlink. find(1) does not follow a symlink given as its starting point, so the logical path matched no file and the gate ended in SKIP, exit 0.
- M2 (`c10b129e`): the root check that every `--exclude` value passes now also runs over the default prune list and the third-party list (`root_prune_hit`): a root named `out`, `build`, `dist`, `vendor`, `node_modules`, `.venv`, `__pycache__`, `third_party`, `site-packages`, or standing at or below `scripts/gates` is refused with exit 2 naming the list and the find alternative (it was pruned whole and ended in SKIP, exit 0). `--include-third-party` switches only the third-party list off.
- L1 (`c7cc0394`, test only): the default-prune and third-party parts of the "would the same scan without the `--exclude` entries find a source file" check (`nocli_prune_expr`) had no case; three mutants of it survived the previous selftest. New case X21 and three paired mutants.
- L4 (`7cef6986`): the `EXCLUDED (explicit --exclude, not scanned)` listing walks with the default and third-party prunes first, so a directory below `node_modules` or `site-packages` is not listed as an exclusion of the flag, its files are not counted, and an `--exclude` that matches only there prints the "matched no directory" NOTE.
- L2 (`39dcf5f7`): `scripts/gates/cm_multitrack_engine_in_constitution.sh` reads env's options the way env does (short-option clusters letter by letter; `-u`/`-C`/`-P` take the rest of the word or else the next word; `--unset`/`--chdir` the next word unless written `=`; `-S` and `--split-string=` carry the interpreter inside the option word, quotes dropped). `#!/usr/bin/env -Spython3`, `-iSpython3`, `-S'python3 -I'`, `--split-string=python3` and `-iu FOO python3` were read as sh; the `VAR=value` skip had no test (removing it survived). 12 PASS and 4 FAIL shebang fixtures and five new mutants.
- L3 (`bb833dfe`): a failing python compile is reported as one line, `<name>:<line>: <ErrorType>: <message>` (name = engine-relative path, message whitespace-collapsed and cut at 200 characters), not the traceback (769 bytes for the test fixture, 487 on the real engine; the consumer's report row cuts at 400 bytes). Verdicts and exit codes are unchanged.
- Not changed: any anchor, `Constitution.md`, the carriers, `scripts/lib`, the workable-items engine, `cm_killpg_pgid_guard_mutation_test.sh` (it runs the scope selftest and so exercises the new cases).

#### VERIFIED (commands run on 2026-11-06 in the scratch clone under `env -i PATH=/usr/bin:/bin` with GNU findutils, output seen)

L2's `VAR=value` mutant (`-*|*=*)` to `-*)`) leaves the unchanged engine mutation test at 53 `META OK`, 0 `META FAIL` (survives); the new test catches it.

## Unreleased — 2026-11-06 — gate fixes for two consumer reds: CM-MULTITRACK-ENGINE-IN-CONSTITUTION reads a python-declaring engine script with python's own grammar; CM-KILLPG-PGID-GUARD gets an explicit, printed `--exclude` flag

**Status of this entry: UNRELEASED; implemented on host evidence only; not VERIFIED-DONE; not accepted.** No tag exists for it. **Independent review round 1 (Opus, xhigh) on `bdc3ac430b01..055890524306` returned NO-GO** (1 HIGH, 2 MEDIUM, 1 LOW-MEDIUM, 2 LOW, 1 INFO); findings 1 to 6 are addressed by the commits `a968ac806` (killpg gate) and `627eb9678` (engine gate) below, finding 7 is recorded under UNCONFIRMED; **round 2 review is pending** and no GO exists. Prepared in a scratch clone at `b939266a13a3` (the pin of the consuming repository) and re-applied on upstream `main` `bdc3ac430b01`; the two code commits apply cleanly on both bases and produce the same gate verdicts on both. Driver: the consuming repository's pre-build run r10 (2026-11-06) failed the rows `CM-MULTITRACK-ENGINE-IN-CONSTITUTION` and `CM-KILLPG-PGID-GUARD`, and both causes sit in the gates shipped here.

#### What changed

- `scripts/gates/cm_multitrack_engine_in_constitution.sh`: the parseability leg reads the interpreter the shebang DECLARES (`bash`, `python`/`pythonN[.M]`, through `env` and its options; every other line keeps the legacy bash-or-sh rule). A python-declaring script is COMPILED with `python3 -I -c 'compile(open(p,"rb").read(), p, "exec", dont_inherit=True)'` (no execution, no bytecode; `ast.parse` was replaced after review round 1 because it accepts what python3 refuses at compile time: a top-level `return`/`break`/`continue`/`yield`/`await`, a bad `nonlocal`, `global` after use, a misplaced or impossible `from __future__`, `def f(a, a)`); a host without python3 FAILS closed. The `env` options that take an argument (`-u`, `-C`, `-P`, `--unset`, `--chdir`) are skipped together with that argument before the interpreter word is read, and the python match is exactly `python`, `pythonN`, `pythonN.M...` (not `python3-config`). A temp file that cannot be created is exit 2 with a message, not a parse failure. Before: `agent_label_frontmatter_check.py` (shebang `python3`) was demanded to pass `sh -n` and failed at line 24. The "clean" line is printed only for a file that parsed clean.
- `scripts/gates/cm_multitrack_engine_in_constitution_mutation_test.sh`: cases 4a to 4d (valid-python-invalid-shell PASS with a control needle, python syntax error FAIL, bash/sh-declaring script with python-only content still FAIL, `env -S python3 -I`, no python3 FAIL); after review round 1 cases 5 to 10: 10 compile-stage fixtures with a control needle each, 14 shebang fixtures (env options with arguments, `python3.12`, `python3-config`, `pythonista`, `#!/bin/sh # python`), isolation cases with control needles (a `PYTHONPATH` `sitecustomize` and a planted working-directory `ast.py` are never executed), the unusable-`TMPDIR` case with a control, eight gate mutants (parser back to `ast.parse`, `-I` dropped, both, any shebang mentioning python, `python*`, `python[0-9]*`, env argument not skipped, mktemp failure ignored), `expect_fail` asserting exit 1 exactly and a stub-gate self-check of that.
- `scripts/gates/cm_killpg_pgid_guard.sh`: `--exclude <glob>` (repeatable, additive to the default prune list, same matching as `KILLPG_GUARD_EXCLUDE`), always printed (`EXCLUDED (explicit --exclude, not scanned): <dir> (N source file(s))` plus the files), a no-match NOTE, exit 2 on an empty, absolute, `..`, wildcard-only or unsafe value, ignored with a NOTE for a single-file root. After review round 1: the values are kept in an array and used as typed (never expanded against the caller's working directory), a value that matches the scan root itself or one of its ancestors (`find -maxdepth 0` on the resolved root; `find` applies `-name`/`-path` to its starting point) is refused with exit 2 instead of pruning the whole scan into a SKIP, an exclusion that leaves no source file to scan although the same scan without it has one is exit 2 (a tree with no source file at all still SKIPs), and the no-match NOTE prints the find test that was applied. `KILLPG_GUARD_EXCLUDE` stays a guarded override (honoured only under `HELIX_TEST_MODE=1`).
- `scripts/gates/cm_killpg_pgid_guard_scope_selftest.sh`: round 9, cases X1 to X8 with their own mutants; after review round 1 round 10, cases X9 to X17 (control, root basename, root tail, ancestor tail, glob matching the root, zero-file exit 2, no-source SKIP kept, cwd holding a matching entry, applied pattern in the NOTE) with eight paired mutants; the existing `nonote` mutant follows the renamed variable.
- Whole-file shellcheck cleanup of the four touched gate scripts (commits `a19bbfa9c` and `84fffdea3`; made on upstream `bdc3ac430b01` only), behaviour unchanged. The review pre-check lints every touched shell script whole, so the two code commits above made all findings of these files count: 26 blocking findings and 2 `disable=` lines without a same-line justification (rule D5), of which 23 findings and both disables already stood at the base `bdc3ac430b01`. Fixed at the cause: `CDPATH=''` (SC1007), `declare exts excludes` before the `cm_gate_env_set` calls that assign them (SC2154), `langx="sh"` (SC2209), `printf '%b' "$BADPY"` (SC2059, byte-identical output), declare and assign apart (SC2155), a quoted grep pattern (SC2062), and a same-line reason on the two existing `disable=` lines. No disable was added and no legacy-exception row was used (its reason classes do not fit these files).
- Not changed: any anchor, `Constitution.md`, the carriers, the generated index and groups, `scripts/anchors`, `scripts/lib`, the workable-items engine.

## Unreleased — 2026-11-05 — §11.4.296 GitHub Issues are a source of workable items (the issues assigned to a declared account are imported on a declared interval, one tracker item per issue, re-checked by content hash, answered by a guarded status write-back and proven by a fail-closed gate; GitHub to tracker only)

**Status of this entry: UNRELEASED; the change is PROVISIONAL-DONE (§11.4.290 labelling), not VERIFIED-DONE.** No tag exists for it. It was prepared in a scratch copy of the constitution at `cb31e32a1a06` (the pin of the consuming repository) by worker GH-RULES of effort BG-20261105-GITHUB-ISSUES-SYNC, on the operator order of 2026-11-05 22:18 +05 and the scope ruling of 22:24 +05 (quoted in the anchor). The push to the remotes and the move of the consuming repository's gitlink are separate steps that the conductor makes.

#### What changed

- **Anchor §11.4.296** (`Constitution.md`, the next free number: the highest heading at `cb31e32a1a06` and at upstream `main` `7f723954` is §11.4.295, and no ref of the local clone holds a §11.4.296): clauses (A) source declaration and direction (GitHub to tracker only: the sync tooling never creates, transfers or exports an issue, and an item without the marker line `GitHub-Issue: <owner>/<repo>#<number>` never receives a comment, a label or a state change), (B) periodic sync and the run record (interval at most 24 hours, five steps, INCONCLUSIVE when offline), (C) one item per issue and no duplicates (pre-existing duplicates are grouped, never deleted), (D) re-check by content hash, (E) status write-back with a consumer-declared closed vocabulary of seven classes and eight guards (default `dry-run`), (F) the external-tracker leg (blocked with a closed reason code, UNCONFIRMED, never synced), (G) derived documents in sync, (H) response time in checkable terms, (I) the done ladder is not bypassed, (J) the fail-closed evidence gate (PASS, FAIL, BLIND exit 2, INCONCLUSIVE exit 3), (K) what the anchor does not change. Refinement notes in §11.4.93, §11.4.148 and §11.4.294.

## Unreleased — 2026-11-05 — CM-ORACLE-STRATEGY-NAMED-AND-INDEPENDENT: frozen-evidence exclusion list (CON-043); baseline 64 -> 0 entries

**Status: UNRELEASED, PROVISIONAL-DONE (§11.4.290), not VERIFIED-DONE; independent review round 2 of max 5: GO-with-follow-ups (§11.4.276(G)), follow-ups CON-049 to CON-053.** The 64 baseline entries were the whole content of 3 archived P1 RED-evidence files in the llmctl submodule (`n_tests.py` 29, `test_d16_d32.py` 20, `test_defects_d01_d15.py` 15). New `config/oracle_frozen_evidence_exclusions.tsv` (TAB: literal path, class from the closed set `frozen-red-evidence`, git blob id as the file's owning repository computes it, reason; required header `# ceiling: N`, currently 3) and a gate change: a listed file whose current blob id equals the recorded one is not scanned; any other state VOIDS the entry (file scanned again and the gate FAILs, even with baseline slack); globs, absolute or `..` paths, unknown classes, bad blob ids, duplicates, a missing ceiling are BLIND (rc 2); more entries than the ceiling FAILs; `--strict` ignores the list; the verdict line gains `excluded=`, `voided=`, `exclusion_entries=`. `config/oracle_ratchet_baseline.txt` now holds 0 entries. New `tests/gates/test_oracle_frozen_exclusions.sh` (paired mutations, plus a part B that proves on the real tree that no runner executes the 3 files). Not changed: the frozen files, the llmctl submodule.

Fix round 2 (independent review round 1 NO-GO, §11.4.276(G)): the RED step of `test_oracle_frozen_exclusions.sh` and of the pre-existing `test_oracle_ratchet_and_consumer_skips.sh` built the "pre-change" gate with `git show HEAD:`, which can never go RED once the change is committed; both now pin the RED gate to a pre-change commit (`ef18fea`, `ca88eda`) and assert the pinned gate lacks the feature. The exclusion block moved above the rule-3 base resolution, so "no candidate test files" is SKIP rc 0 again without a remote main or with a bad `--base`. The blob id is computed by the owning repository (`git -C <dir> hash-object --path=<name>`: its object format and checkout filters, as `git status` compares), so a git-clean `core.autocrlf=true`, `eol=crlf` or `ident` checkout, or a caller cwd inside a sha256 repository, no longer voids an entry; 64-hex (sha256) ids are accepted. Stricter parsing: exactly 4 non-empty TAB columns (a doubled TAB is BLIND), no `./`, `//`, `/.` segments, an unreadable list is BLIND naming the cause. `--write-baseline` / `--adopt-new-files` refuse (rc 1, `reason=exclusions_voided`) while an entry is voided instead of printing PASS after a FAIL line. The reviewer's mutants RM1-RM10 are adopted in the test; part B now FAILs when an inspected file or directory is missing and carries class-matched control needles.

Fix round 2b (commit f8a45db): an explicit or default `--exclusions` path that is not a regular file (a missing path, a directory, a FIFO, a dangling symlink, `/dev/null`) now means "no list" instead of BLIND, so `--exclusions /dev/null` is the documented way to scan without exclusions (the control used by the RED proof: FAIL, ratchet total=64). This only disables exclusions, so the gate scans more files, never fewer; a symlink to the real list is followed. The reviewer confirmed it on the real tree for every non-regular shape. Diagnosis of a typo'd explicit path is tracked as CON-052.

## Unreleased — 2026-11-04 — workable-items engine: reproduction and investigation state columns (schema v8), `states import` / `states freeze`, validator and render support (the engine side of §11.4.294(A), (C), (D), (F), (H))

**Status of this entry: UNRELEASED; the change is PROVISIONAL-DONE (§11.4.290 labelling), not VERIFIED-DONE.** No tag exists for it. It was prepared in a scratch clone (patch `constitution_workable_items_states.patch`, 21 files) and landed here as its own commit on top of the §11.4.294 / §11.4.295 anchors commit, on the upstream `main` tip `820a902`; the push to the remotes is a separate step. Host evidence only: no consumer database was opened by this landing, and the consumer's own tracker database is still at schema v7 until the new binary first opens it.

#### What changed (all under `scripts/workable-items/`)

- **Schema v8**: two columns on `items`, `reproduction_state` and `investigation_state` (closed vocabularies, default `UNSET`), plus the record detail columns, in `schema.sql` and the embedded `cmd/workable-items/schema_embed.sql`; a lossless v7-to-v8 migration; an `Updated` history event that carries the states reason on `update` and on `states import`.
- **Round trip**: the `**Reproduction:**` and `**Investigation:**` body lines are parsed into the columns and canonicalised back from them (`states.go`, `parse.go`, `export.go`, `sync.go`); a line whose remainder is not a closed-set head is legacy prose and is never interpreted or rewritten; a body line that disagrees with its column is a `validate` / `diff` desync finding.
- **Subcommands**: `states import --db <p> --tsv <file> --by <who>` (one transaction, all-or-nothing, idempotent, `--dry-run`) and `states freeze --db <p> --by <who>` (`--dry-run`); `add` / `update` take the state flags (`--states-updated-by`, `--states-updated-at`, and the state and detail flags listed by `workable-items help`).
- **Closure gate (§11.4.294(D))**: implemented but NOT armed. It is armed only by a one-time `states freeze`; this landing did not run `states freeze` against any database, so the armed-only checks are inert (C2: a Bug closed Fixed / Implemented / Completed with `reproduction_state` `UNSET`; C3: a defect closed Obsolete without one of the four results, or with reason `not-reproducible` without `not-reproducible-with-evidence`). The always-on check C1 is active: an item closed Fixed / Implemented / Completed that carries a reproduction record must be labelled REPRODUCED (an UNREPRODUCED item never reaches VERIFIED-DONE).
- **Tracked binaries**: `bin/workable-items` and `bin/workable-items-linux` rebuilt byte-identical with `GOPROXY=off go build -trimpath -buildvcs=false -ldflags=-buildid=` (go1.26.4, linux/amd64), sha256 `9edd2dc3dfe33dd33ad4583256bd8c96650cf7edd01b77da5022d701f2a70e2c` (three builds, two worktrees, one hash; the same hash as the 2026-11-03 rehearsal). `bin/.source.sha256` re-recorded as `fa9542151c85193ce614e86e18f3cbedcdf001e86c5a9ad10017aa87b1273a7b`: the formula was recovered by experiment and reproduces the value recorded at commit `10c3258` (`2b0a50f85b12c8f13c886c39faacde31ba34b2338b9bed589beb44db1d233d84`) exactly: `cd scripts/workable-items && find . -type f \( -name '*.go' -o -name go.mod -o -name go.sum \) -not -path './bin/*' | LC_ALL=en_US.UTF-8 sort | xargs sha256sum | sha256sum` (80 files; the order is locale dependent). The two earlier binary updates (`974f35d`, `09dacd6`) left this file stale because the formula was not locatable in the repository; a grep for `source.sha256` and `BINARY-FRESH` found no gate that reads it in this repository or in the consumer's `scripts/`, `tests/` and pre-build suite (UNCONFIRMED that none exists elsewhere).
- **Spelling of the sixth reproduction value (conductor ruling 2026-11-04, decided; one spelling everywhere).** The engine stores, renders and quotes the long token `blocked-no-device-with-recorded-blocker`, the §11.4.292(B) spelling; the short `blocked-no-device` is an input alias only (the value of `--reproduction-state`, a `reproduction_state` cell of a `states import` TSV), normalised to the long form before storage, never stored, never rendered, and not a closed-set head of a `**Reproduction:**` body line (`validate` reports it as `not a canonical Markdown result token`). No engine source and no test changed for this ruling: the engine already behaved this way, so the binary hash stays `9edd2dc3dfe33dd33ad4583256bd8c96650cf7edd01b77da5022d701f2a70e2c` and `bin/.source.sha256` stays `fa9542151c85193ce614e86e18f3cbedcdf001e86c5a9ad10017aa87b1273a7b`; the change is the anchor text of §11.4.294 in the preceding commit (which first listed the short form as the column value) and its gate. Live probe on the tracked binary (2026-11-04): `update --reproduction-state blocked-no-device` leaves `items.reproduction_state = blocked-no-device-with-recorded-blocker`, the history reason `reproduction UNSET -> blocked-no-device-with-recorded-blocker`, the body line and the summary cell in the long form (identical to the same update with the long flag value); a body line edited to start with the short form makes `validate` exit 1; an unknown spelling (`blocked-no-dev`) is refused with the closed set listed and the stored value is untouched.
- **Documentation (review finding F-2):** `scripts/workable-items/README.md` (revision 5) now documents the states: the two closed vocabularies, the one-spelling rule, the body records, the `add` / `update` flags, `states import` (TSV header and semantics), `states freeze`, the closure checks C1 to C3, the migration and the honest boundaries (no evidence sha256 is recorded or re-verified; no automatic demotion of lost evidence; the commit-level transitions, the regression reopen and the tracker mirror are consumer work). Every command line in it was run against the tracked binary on a scratch database.

#### VERIFIED (commands run on 2026-11-04 on the landing tree, output seen)

- `go test -count=1 -v ./...` in `scripts/workable-items`: 573 `--- PASS`, 0 `--- FAIL`, 6 `--- SKIP` (the baseline tree at `1e9a2c4` gives 545 PASS, 0 FAIL, the same 6 SKIPs: four need the consumer's real `docs/` trackers or `/tmp/atm_fixed_real.md` and therefore did not run here (`TestStatusDesyncAndDuplicateHistoryRows_StayZero`, `TestParseFixed_Real_FullFile_Count`, `TestRoundTrip_RealDocs`, `TestValidate_OK_RealDocs`), two are GREEN-only guards that skip by design (`TestGapA_RenderRepresentationKeyIsLoadBearing`, `TestGapB_PipeRowSynthesisIsParseable`)).
- `python3 -I mutation/states_mutation_runner.py` → `SUMMARY killed=19 survived=0 verdict=PASS` (control passes, every mutant killed).
- `bash scripts/fastcycle/tests/test_shipped_workable_items_us3_red.sh` run through a consumer layout against the new shipped binary → `SUMMARY: all hold` (also on the baseline); `bash tests/gates/test_fastcycle_real_db.sh` → `RESULT: PASS` on the earlier landing base `45639d2` (2026-11-04), but `RESULT: FAIL` on the landing base `820a902` (and on `5f05ca9`) with output byte-identical to the pure upstream tip `820a902` run without this change: its select_sample suite reports `NOT ok mutation SS_N3_negative_gap_retroactive could not be applied (anchor drifted)` and the same for `R3_blind_guard_import_error_only`, because the upstream merge `378f714` changed `scripts/fastcycle/cycle/select_sample.py` (both mutation anchors are present at `45639d2`, absent at `378f714`, `5f05ca9` and `820a902`) while `scripts/fastcycle/tests/test_select_sample_r5_regression.sh` kept the old anchor text. That is an upstream defect outside this change; this change leaves it untouched and is no worse than the baseline on it.

#### PROVISIONAL / UNCONFIRMED

- UNCONFIRMED: the behaviour of the new binary on the consumer's real tracker database. A rehearsal on a copy (2026-11-03, 66 closed items would be exempted by `states freeze --dry-run`, 0 unconditional closure problems, real files unchanged) exists but was not repeated here.
- The defaults stay `UNSET`; no item was assessed; the closure gate stays unarmed; the tracker mirror (§11.4.294(I)) and the derived-document columns (§11.4.294(H)) are consumer work (OWED-GATE-178 / OWED-GATE-179).
- No independent §11.4.209 review has run on this change.

- `CM-UNREFERENCED-GATE-BOUND-OR-RETIRED` against a scratch consumer layout (`--root` the layout, `constitution` symlinked to the tree): `UNBOUND=50` on the upstream tip `820a902` and `UNBOUND=50` with both anchors landed, the identical set of 50 gate names (the gate files grow from 366 to 374, the eight new ones all bound; the figure was 48 on the earlier scratch base where it was first measured on 2026-11-03); `bash scripts/gates/cm_canonical_root_clarity.sh` against the same layout → PASS.

#### PROVISIONAL (not verified by any command)

- That the anchor text is complete and unambiguous: no independent §11.4.209 review round has run.
- The forensic quote is the conductor's rendering of the operator's 2026-11-03 ruling "Substitute app, same verdict"; the operator's own message was not available to the author and is not quoted as verbatim.
- The anchor adds mechanics the ruling does not spell out and that therefore need the operator's confirmation: a control probe from the same egress that separates a region refusal from an outage, a substitute chain declared before the run, an equal criteria-set hash for the original and the substituted run, and the rule that a test whose subject is the restricted app has no substitute and keeps the §11.4.3 SKIP-with-reason. UNCONFIRMED that the operator intends exactly these.
- No consuming project carries a substitute-chain registry, a criteria-set hash or a probe-record writer yet; the constitution ships none (OWED-GATE-180).

### Added

- **§11.4.295 — Substitute app, same verdict.** (A) The app's content API is probed on every run from the run's own egress; a refusal is `GEO_RESTRICTED` only with a refusal signature from the consumer's closed list (default: timeout, HTTP 403, HTTP 451, a refused connection, a region-block page), a successful control probe from the same egress (otherwise `network_unreachable_external`) and a captured record; without the record the instrument is INCONCLUSIVE. (B) The SAME test (same steps, oracles, thresholds, windows, run count) runs with a substitute app taken from a chain the consumer declared in advance for the test's capability class; the substitute is a different app, never a different test. (C) Same verdict: both runs record the sha256 of the criteria set and the two must be equal; the substitute's verdict is the test's verdict; `GEO_RESTRICTED` itself is never reported as a failure, never a PASS and never the final verdict while a reachable substitute exists; weaker criteria is a PASS-bluff. (D) The report line `GEO_RESTRICTED(<original app>) -> <substitute app>: <verdict>`; a substituted run proves the platform path, not the restricted app's own behaviour, and a test whose subject is the restricted app keeps the SKIP-with-reason. (E) An exhausted chain ends as the §11.4.3 SKIP-with-reason `geo_restricted`. (F) A substituted run is a verdict-bearing run in the §11.4.293 comparison; an exhausted chain SKIPPED where the baseline was PASS stays `baseline-pass-lost`. (G) What is not changed. This answers the open question recorded for the consuming project in the §11.4.293 entry below.
- **Refinement notes** (dated 2026-11-03) at the end of the §11.4.69, §11.4.107, §11.4.117, §11.4.143 and §11.4.293 blocks, each pointing at §11.4.295 without restating it; the closed SKIP reason set of §11.4.69 (`geo_restricted` included) is unchanged.
- **Gates:** `CM-COVENANT-114-295-PROPAGATION` (generated from a new data-pack row, with its generated mutation test; seam row `11.4.295<TAB>pre-build<TAB>CM-COVENANT-114-295-PROPAGATION`) and `CM-GEO-SUBSTITUTE-ANCHOR-WIRED` (`scripts/gates/cm_geo_substitute_anchor_wired.sh`, 137 checks) with its paired mutation test. Recommended item-level gate `CM-GEO-SUBSTITUTE-SAME-VERDICT` (`OWED-GATE-180`) registered as a deferral (`gate_ledger_deferrals.tsv`, `docs/owed_gate_implementations.md` revision 19).
- **Hook binding:** `scripts/post_update_hook.sh` step 5b also runs `CM-GEO-SUBSTITUTE-ANCHOR-WIRED` against the constitution root; `tests/test_post_update_hub_gates.sh` gains an eighth stub gate and the cases GEO-FAIL, GEO-BLIND, GEO-SKIP (21 cases).

### Changed

- **Carriers:** identical compact §11.4.295 entry appended at the end of `CLAUDE.md` (revision 94), `AGENTS.md` (91), `QWEN.md` (88), `GEMINI.md` (62); `Constitution.md` revision 91.
- **Anchor machinery:** `scripts/anchors/anchor_lib.py` maps §11.4.295 to group `testing-and-tdd`; `scripts/anchors/tests/test_query_us2.py` universal count 192 to 193; index, groups, progressive split and manifest regenerated with the repository's own generators.
- **README** revision 24: facts, group, clause and release tables re-measured; badge row regenerated.

### Honest boundaries

- The gate proves the RULE is present, complete and wired; it does not prove that any test probes first, that any substitute chain is declared, that the criteria hashes are equal or that a substituted run exercised the same platform path (OWED-GATE-180).
- The control probe bounds, and does not eliminate, the chance that a refusal is a defect rather than a region rule (§11.4.118).

It was prepared in a scratch clone of the constitution (branch `t1-const-text-20261103`) on top of the §11.4.293 commit `c5f51cb5`, then landed on the upstream `main` tip `820a902` (the v73.3 content, the speckit-bridge N1-N5 follow-up, the baseline_replay trusted-parent work, the runner-canon fixes and the deterministic full-suite runner; the 3-way overlap was `CHANGELOG.md` and `README.md` (the badge row, regenerated, against the earlier upstream tip; a different `README.md` line against the full-suite runner commit, merged without conflict), and no anchor, group or generated file moved upstream) in a second scratch clone (branch `t1-landing-20261104`); the push to the remotes is a separate step. ### Added

- **§11.4.294 — Reproduction and investigation states are workable-item fields.** (A) Two fields on every item, default `UNSET` (never assessed): `reproduction_state` (`UNSET` / `UNREPRODUCED` / `reproduced-live` / `reproduced-host-fixture` / `not-reproducible-with-evidence` / `blocked-no-device-with-recorded-blocker`) and `investigation_state` (`UNSET` / `not-started` / `in-progress` / `root-cause-found-with-evidence` / `root-cause-unconfirmed` / `closed-no-defect`); the database is the source, the `**Reproduction:**` and `**Investigation:**` body lines are the mirror and carry the details; ONE SPELLING of every state (the sixth reproduction value is the §11.4.292(B) token `blocked-no-device-with-recorded-blocker` in the column, the body line, the history and every derived document; the short `blocked-no-device` is an input alias only). (B) Mapping to the §11.4.292 labels. (C) A state above the defaults needs a resolving evidence path and sha256; lost evidence demotes the state, visibly. (D) Nine lifecycle transitions T1 to T9 (a fix commit needs `reproduced-live`, `reproduced-host-fixture` or `blocked-no-device-with-recorded-blocker` and a proven root cause; VERIFIED-DONE needs `reproduced-live`, or `reproduced-host-fixture` on a `host-only` surface, and `root-cause-found-with-evidence`; `Obsolete (not-reproducible)` needs `not-reproducible-with-evidence` and `closed-no-defect`). (E) Applicability: a defect item only. (F) PROVISIONAL-DONE versus VERIFIED-DONE matrix and the reopen rule when the reproduction regresses (`Reproduction-Regress:` / `Investigation-Regress:` lines; lost evidence does not reopen). (G) A state change is not a status change. (H) Derived documents quote the parent item's two states (plain trailing columns, never a sort key, never in a numbers-only count cell, never a second source). (I) The external-tracker mirror (one-way, description header lines and tags, fingerprint extended, sink-side read-back, offline = `UNCONFIRMED`). (J) What is not changed.
- **Refinement notes** (dated 2026-11-03) at the end of the §11.4.34, §11.4.93, §11.4.148, §11.4.290 and §11.4.292 blocks, each pointing at §11.4.294 without restating it.
- **Gates:** `CM-COVENANT-114-294-PROPAGATION` (generated from a new data-pack row, with its generated mutation test; seam row `11.4.294<TAB>pre-build<TAB>CM-COVENANT-114-294-PROPAGATION`) and `CM-STATE-FIELDS-ANCHOR-WIRED` (`scripts/gates/cm_state_fields_anchor_wired.sh`, 244 checks: 41 canon marker phrases, the two six-value vocabularies (the sixth reproduction value in its long spelling), the one-spelling checks (the short `blocked-no-device` exactly once per block in the canon, the four mirrors, the lifecycle skill, the group file and the progressive rule file, each time as the input-alias statement), the nine transition rows, 6 escape-hatch flags, 23 cross-references, five back-pointers, four carrier mirrors, the item template, both skills, index, group, rule file, catalog, data-pack row, wrappers, seam binding, three deferral rows read by their OWED id, hook binding) with its paired mutation test. Recommended item-level gates `CM-STATE-FIELDS-LIFECYCLE` (`OWED-GATE-177`), `CM-STATE-PROPAGATION` (`OWED-GATE-178`) and `CM-STATE-TRACKER-MIRROR` (`OWED-GATE-179`) registered as deferrals (`gate_ledger_deferrals.tsv`, `docs/owed_gate_implementations.md` revision 18).
- **Hook binding:** `scripts/post_update_hook.sh` step 5b also runs `CM-STATE-FIELDS-ANCHOR-WIRED` against the constitution root; `tests/test_post_update_hub_gates.sh` gains a seventh stub gate and the cases STATEFIELDS-FAIL, STATEFIELDS-BLIND, STATEFIELDS-SKIP (18 cases).
- **Item template:** `scripts/reporting/report_item.sh` renders `**Investigation:** not-started (§11.4.294)` under the `**Reproduction:**` line of every `Bug`.
- **Skills:** `skills/workable-item-lifecycle/` version 1.4.0 (the two vocabularies, the transition table, the regress lines, the item body block), `skills/reporting-workable-items/` version 1.2.0 (the Investigation line and the field mapping).

### Changed

- **Carriers:** identical compact §11.4.294 entry appended at the end of `CLAUDE.md` (revision 93), `AGENTS.md` (90), `QWEN.md` (87), `GEMINI.md` (61); `Constitution.md` revision 90.

The short `blocked-no-device`, which the first draft of clause (A) listed as the column value (with the long form as the body token), is now only an INPUT ALIAS: a command-line flag value or an import-file cell may carry it, the engine normalises it to the long form before anything is stored, and it is never stored, never rendered and never the head of a `**Reproduction:**` body line. Changed in clause (A) (row and `CHECK` clause), clause (B) (table row and body-token map, which is now the identity map for the five non-`none` tokens), clause (C), rows T3, T5 and T7, the clause (F) matrix, the four carriers, the lifecycle skill, and the generated index, group file and progressive rule file (regenerated, not hand-edited; the index now records the real parent commit `820a902` instead of the `c5f51cb` of the first scratch clone). `CM-STATE-FIELDS-ANCHOR-WIRED` pins it (see Gates).

#### PROVISIONAL (not verified by any command)

- That the anchor text is complete and unambiguous: no independent §11.4.209 review round has run.
