- No consuming project carries a comparator or a `Roadmap-Item:` trailer check yet; the constitution ships neither (OWED-GATE-175, OWED-GATE-176).

### Added

- **§11.4.293 — Scope discipline and image no-worse proof.** (A) All work serves a workable item of the main Roadmap and stays inside the codebase context of the issue it fixes; every commit names its item with a `Roadmap-Item: <id>` trailer, and every path outside that context carries an `Out-of-scope: <path> — <reason> — <justification>` trailer whose reason is exactly one of `fix-gate-or-test`, `generated-regeneration`, `upstream-merge`, `operator-order`; anything else is split into its own item or reverted. (B)-(E) Every produced flashing image is LIVE-tested and compared, test by test, with the baseline run of the previously flashed image on the same board serial, harness, suite and command, both image identities recorded; closed verdict `NO-WORSE-PROVEN` / `REGRESSED` (reason codes `baseline-pass-lost`, `issue-count-higher`, `targeted-flip-missing`) / `INCOMPARABLE-BASELINE-MISSING`; a baseline `PASS` that is `INCONCLUSIVE`, `SKIPPED`, `BLIND`, `ERROR` or absent on the new image is a regression; new tests are reported separately; every image that fixes anything is more stable (targeted §11.4.292(E) flips present), a purely new-feature image is still `NO-WORSE-PROVEN` on every pre-existing test. (F) `REGRESSED` and `INCOMPARABLE-BASELINE-MISSING` refuse every release and its tag; a consumer-declared dev-class tag may still be created, its changelog stating the verdict first. Composes §11.4.40, §11.4.114, §11.4.135, §11.4.200, §11.4.236, §11.4.290 and §11.4.292 without restating them.
- **Refinement notes** (dated 2026-11-03) at the end of the §11.4.40, §11.4.114 and §11.4.135 blocks, each pointing at §11.4.293 without restating it.
- **Gates:** `CM-COVENANT-114-293-PROPAGATION` (generated from a new data-pack row, with its generated mutation test; seam row `11.4.293<TAB>pre-build<TAB>CM-COVENANT-114-293-PROPAGATION`) and `CM-SCOPE-AND-IMAGE-NOWORSE-ANCHOR-WIRED` (`scripts/gates/cm_scope_and_image_noworse_anchor_wired.sh`, 171 checks: 34 canon marker phrases, the four out-of-scope reasons and the three verdicts in the canon, the carriers and the lifecycle skill, 10 escape-hatch flags, 31 cross-references, three back-pointers, four carrier mirrors, index, group, rule file, catalog, data-pack row, wrappers, seam binding, both deferral rows read by their OWED id, hook binding) with its paired mutation test. Recommended item-level gates `CM-COMMIT-NAMES-ROADMAP-ITEM` (`OWED-GATE-175`) and `CM-IMAGE-NO-WORSE-PROVEN` (`OWED-GATE-176`) registered as deferrals (`gate_ledger_deferrals.tsv`, `docs/owed_gate_implementations.md` revision 17), NOT claimed implemented.
- **Hook binding:** `scripts/post_update_hook.sh` step 5b also runs `CM-SCOPE-AND-IMAGE-NOWORSE-ANCHOR-WIRED` against the constitution root; `tests/test_post_update_hub_gates.sh` gains a sixth stub gate and the cases NOWORSE-FAIL, NOWORSE-BLIND, NOWORSE-SKIP (15 cases).
- **Skill:** `skills/workable-item-lifecycle/` version 1.3.0 (the `Roadmap-Item:` / `Out-of-scope:` trailers with the four reasons, and the image comparison rule).

### Changed

- **Carriers:** identical compact §11.4.293 entry appended at the end of `CLAUDE.md` (revision 92), `AGENTS.md` (89), `QWEN.md` (86), `GEMINI.md` (60); `Constitution.md` revision 89.

### Honest boundaries

- A no-worse verdict is bounded by the suite: a defect no test exercises is invisible to it (§11.4.118).
- Open question recorded for the consuming project: a project rule that treats a geo-restricted content test as "never a failure" meets clause (C), where a baseline-PASS test that is SKIPPED on the new image is `baseline-pass-lost`; the constitution does not decide that project rule.

#### PROVISIONAL (not verified by any command)

- That the anchor text is complete and unambiguous: no independent §11.4.209 review round has run.
- The forensic quote is the conductor's rendering of the operator's 2026-11-03 mandate ("verbatim intent"); the operator's own message was not available to the author and is not quoted as verbatim.
- No item in any consuming project carries a `Reproduction:` record yet; the engine does not parse the line (OWED-GATE-174).

### Added

- **§11.4.292 — Reproduce first.** Every issue being fixed is reproduced LIVE (§11.4.289 meaning) before any cause hypothesis is acted on or any fix is written. Closed result vocabulary `reproduced-live` / `reproduced-host-fixture` / `not-reproducible-with-evidence` / `blocked-no-device-with-recorded-blocker`, item labels `REPRODUCED` / `UNREPRODUCED` (`reproduced-host-fixture` is `REPRODUCED` only on a `host-only` surface). The record rides on a `**Reproduction:**` line (template default `**Reproduction:** none — UNREPRODUCED`) with the exact command, the machine-written RED (path, sha256, timestamp, ids), the build / artifact fingerprint read from the target, the target identity (board serial, rig, service or fixture sha256) and the symptom signature. Under blocked reproduction a fix lands only as PROVISIONAL-DONE labelled `UNREPRODUCED` and cannot reach VERIFIED-DONE; the promoting GREEN must use the same command, target identity and signature; a non-reproduction needs a positive control needle and never licenses a fix; the root cause is a FACT only after a decisive cause-toggles-signature experiment; the fix ships with a deterministic live coverage test (a `host-substitute` only when live is impossible, labelled). Composes §11.4.6, §11.4.43, §11.4.102, §11.4.115, §11.4.146, §11.4.199, §11.4.289 and §11.4.290 without restating them.
- **Refinement notes** (dated 2026-11-03) at the end of the §11.4.43, §11.4.115, §11.4.146 and §11.4.199 blocks, each pointing at §11.4.292 without restating it.
- **Gates:** `CM-COVENANT-114-292-PROPAGATION` (generated by `scripts/gates/covenant_propagation_wrappers_generate.sh` from a new data-pack row, with its generated mutation test; seam row `11.4.292<TAB>pre-build<TAB>CM-COVENANT-114-292-PROPAGATION` in `rule_seam_bindings.tsv`) and `CM-REPRO-FIRST-ANCHOR-WIRED` (`scripts/gates/cm_repro_first_anchor_wired.sh`, 174 checks: canon block with 41 marker phrases, the four-value result vocabulary, 10 escape-hatch flags and 32 cross-references, four back-pointers, four carrier mirrors, the item template, both workable-item skills, index, group, rule file, catalog, data-pack row, wrappers, seam binding, the deferral row read by its OWED id, hook binding) with its paired mutation test `cm_repro_first_anchor_wired_mutation_test.sh`. Recommended item-level gate `CM-REPRODUCTION-RECORD-BEFORE-FIX` registered as deferral `OWED-GATE-174` (`gate_ledger_deferrals.tsv`, `docs/owed_gate_implementations.md` revision 16), NOT claimed implemented.
- **Hook binding:** `scripts/post_update_hook.sh` step 5b also runs `CM-REPRO-FIRST-ANCHOR-WIRED` against the constitution root; `tests/test_post_update_hub_gates.sh` gains a fifth stub gate and the cases REPRO-FAIL, REPRO-BLIND, REPRO-SKIP (12 cases).

- Gate repairs (the VERIFIED test above covers the repair test file): oracle and dangerous-combination gates prune third-party by default (opt-in flag to include), prose `oracle:` no longer parsed as a strategy, the `assert False` / `except: pass` test idiom is skipped; 13/13 mutants killed (agent-reported), 59 ok (verified above).
- Badges: static badges data file `config/static_badges.list`; dotted tags (`v73.1`) accepted; `lib_badge_row` treats linked badges as badges (before, `cm_readme_badge_row_at_top` failed and 3 badge gates passed vacuously). Verified above: 64/0 and `--check` rc 0.
- Installer `cases_f` set and mutants MV, BAK, BAKORIG, BAKORDER, TRAP, QUOTE, PYNUM, PYNAN, BOM, JQNAN in `tests/constitution_cmd/test_install_cli_agent_plugins_ownership.sh` (run result not recorded when this entry was written).

#### KNOWN OPEN

1. Canon first-party oracle gate: 414 unannotated tests; the ceiling baseline value is an operator decision (§11.4.66).
2. Dangerous-combination: 150 first-party hits triaged as 0 true defects (`docs/design/canon_self_scan_triage_2026_10_31.md`).
3. Operator decisions pending: default scan root (superproject vs canon); whether `.gitmodules` own-org submodules should be pruned or only `third_party: true`; applicability of `CM-CODEGRAPH-MCP-HEALTH` and `CM-OPENDESIGN-UI-SYSTEM` to the canon.
4. The §11.4.40 full-suite retest was not run for this patch.
5. 4 NOT-ok cases in `scripts/fastcycle/tests` (`test_select_sample_r5_regression` S3/S5/S6, `test_reopen_rate_red` control needle), reported environmental (live tracker DB / sqlite3 unavailable) but NOT proven by a clean-HEAD comparison.
6. `tests/layout/test_consumer_trust_lock.sh` failed once (39/1) and passed on re-run (40/0); cause unknown. A follow-up attempt to reproduce it ran the test 12 times sequentially and 6 times concurrently (all in per-run sandboxes) and got `PASS=40 FAIL=0` 18 of 18 times, so the single failure is UNCONFIRMED and not reproduced; the failing assertion was never captured.
7. The extensions engine remains experimental and dormant; ownership redesign and opencode/kimi/pi wiring are planned, not done.
8. The carried v73 follow-ups listed in the v73 entry below.

### Changed

- README Version badge regenerated with `scripts/badges/compute_badges.sh` (not hand-typed) from this heading; dotted tags such as `v73.1` are now accepted.

### Fixed — `scripts/install_action_prefix.sh` (review of 4d39b88; PROVISIONAL, see status)

- **Python fallback number fidelity (important).** Numbers are carried as their original token, so `1e400`, `1e-400`, `1.0000000000000000001`, `1.5e3`, big integers and `-0.0` round-trip textually (before: `Infinity`, `0.0`, `1.0`, `1500.0` written back, rc 0). `NaN`/`Infinity` are refused (`parse_constant`), the output is dumped with `allow_nan=False`, re-parsed strictly and compared with the in-memory result before the rename. A UTF-8 BOM is now REFUSED on BOTH paths (jq used to accept it); the jq path also REFUSES `NaN`/`Infinity` (jq turns them into `null` / `1.797e308`; checked with jq 1.8.1 only, other jq versions UNCONFIRMED). The jq path still normalises number spelling itself (for example `1.5e3` becomes `1500`); the textual guarantee is for the python path.
- **`.bak` order and policy (minor).** Validation and merge happen first into a temp file; a refused run writes nothing, so it never creates or clobbers `.bak`. The `.bak` is ORIGINAL-PRESERVING: created once from the pre-install file, never overwritten by later runs (delete it by hand to refresh; an existing `.bak` of any content is kept).
- **Temp-file cleanup (minor).** Own temp files (`.settings.new.*`, `.settings.bak.*`, `.settings.seed.*`) are removed on EXIT, TERM (rc 143) and INT (rc 130); a signal is handled after the running `jq`/`python3` child finishes; `kill -9` cannot be handled and may leave them behind.
- **Hook command quoting (minor, pre-existing).** A hook command containing anything outside `A-Za-z0-9_@%+=:,./-` (a space or quote in the path) is single-quoted; safe paths stay unquoted so existing installs still match (idempotent). Before, a constitution path with a space made `sh -c` fail with rc 127.
- **Very deep nesting (nit).** Both paths now print a `REFUSED` message (python no longer raises a traceback; the jq merge failure message now says `REFUSED`). Documented: a read-only (0444) `settings.json` is still rewritten (rename needs only the directory) and keeps its mode.
- Left as is: the `$settings.tmp` containment check is unused for writes (we use `mktemp`) but is covered by existing tests (M1, H1), so it stays as a harmless guard.
- Tests: `tests/constitution_cmd/test_install_cli_agent_plugins_ownership.sh` gains the `cases_f` set (number fidelity, NaN/Infinity/BOM, `.bak` order and original-preserving, 0444, deep nesting, TERM/INT cleanup, spaced and quoted path) and mutants MV, BAK, BAKORIG, BAKORDER, TRAP, QUOTE, PYNUM, PYNAN, BOM, JQNAN killed by it. Status: PROVISIONAL until the final suite runs below are recorded.

## helixconstitution-v73 — 2026-10-31 — one-command installer, updater, versioned setup prompt, decisions wizard, Gitea publisher, submodules/constitution standard

**Status of this entry: RELEASED as `helixconstitution-v73` (2026-10-31; normal release, provisional items labelled per §11.4.290).** **VERIFIED:** installer, updater, prompt and decisions-wizard suites and gates with their paired mutation tests; the `/constitution` command suite (289/0); the CLI-agent plugin ownership suite (187/0) with reviewer-style mutants killed; layout mutation slices M60–M117 (58 killed, 0 survivors); independent Opus reviews of the docs and installer rounds (GO-with-follow-ups). **PROVISIONAL (background verification continues):** the full `mutation_test_constitution_cmd.sh` and `tests/decisions/test_own_mutants.sh` re-runs, layout mutation slices after M117, and live end-to-end installs from every upstream. **KNOWN OPEN follow-ups:** the extensions engine (`scripts/constitution_ext/`) is experimental and dormant (mutant `R12-final-bound` survives its mutation suite); `tests/jev/test_review_fixes_r5.sh` is not hermetic; the auto-committed submodule pin commits need review; the installer's check-then-write (TOCTOU) limit is narrowed, not closed. The documentation was reconciled on 2026-10-31 with the scripts' `--help` output and source; the decisions-wizard tutorial (steps 1 to 5 and the weakening refusal) was run in a throwaway project; the committed transcript `docs/qa/decisions_wizard_tutorial_run.txt` is the only evidence of that run. No live end-to-end install and no live LLM/agent run was made for this documentation pass. The `/constitution` command is implemented and wired for claude, gemini, qwen and codex (`scripts/constitution_cmd/constitution_cmd.sh`, skill `skills/constitution`, generated shims, installed by `scripts/install_cli_agent_plugins.sh` via `scripts/post_update_hook.sh`); opencode, kimi and pi are not wired. The extensions engine (`scripts/constitution_ext/`) sits behind `/constitution extensions ...`; it is experimental, dormant and not independently GO'd, and its installer/post-update wiring and live-agent behaviour are UNCONFIRMED. Verification evidence belongs to the commits and reports that land it, not to this text.

### Added

- `scripts/install_constitution.sh`: a curl-installable, single-file installer (ADD / UPDATE / LEGACY mode detection, agent detection and headless run, deterministic post-verify, backups under `.constitution-install-backup/<timestamp>/`, never pushes). Raw-URL snippets for the github, gitlab, gitlab-vasic, gitflic and gitverse upstreams are at the top of the README, exactly as `--print-snippets` prints them (the `github-vasic` mirror returned 404 and `m3w` is private, so neither has a snippet). Flags beyond the basics: `--upstream-url`, `--ssh`, `--edit-agent-docs`, `--register-hooks`, `--run-setup`, `--commit`, `--git-init`, `--restore DIR`, `--detect-agents [--json]`, `--detect-layout`, `--print-snippets`; exit codes 0 to 6 (plus 130 / 143 on interrupt; 6 = agent success unverified). Agent driving is only unit-class tested; provider aliases, kimi, opencode and pi are UNCONFIRMED.
- `scripts/decisions/constitution_decisions.sh`: decisions wizard for the Jev decision layer; local overrides in `.constitution/decisions.yml` that inherit from the constitution, locked rules cannot be weakened (exit 4), backups under `.constitution-install-backup/<UTC>-decisions/`, exit codes 0 to 5; never runs `bootstrap.sh`, no network, no LLM.
- Installer `--allow-unrestricted-agent` (required for `kimi`, `opencode`, `pi`, which run with their own default permissions; refused with exit 3 when non-interactive without it), a deny list for the Claude family (web tools and `git push` forms), and a post-run no-push check (a detected push fails the run with exit 5; `HELIX_INSTALLER_TEST=1` enables test seams only).
- `scripts/update_constitution.sh`: fast-forward-only updater that works for both layouts and refuses on local changes.
- Installer hardening reflected in the docs (from `install_constitution.sh --help` and `update_constitution.sh --help`, 2026-10-29): bash 4.4 or newer; blobless clone with full-clone fallback; refusal (exit 3) to install into the constitution repository itself, with `--not-constitution-repo` for projects that only use it; `--allow-insecure-url` (http and git URLs refused otherwise); exit code 6 (agent ran but success UNVERIFIED, `opencode` / `pi`); `--max-budget-usd` as a total cap (greater than 0, at most 50); `--model` / `--effort` refused for `kimi`, `opencode`, `pi`; git run with a sanitised environment (inherited `GIT_*` such as `GIT_SSH_COMMAND` dropped; ssh `BatchMode=yes`); credentials removed from printed URLs and query values shown as `REDACTED`; stricter `--restore` rules; a stale-aware install lock; `--detect-layout` also prints `constitution-repo`. The updater's `--check` and `--dry-run` still fetch; default target is the newest release tag `helixconstitution-vN(.N)*`, never a pre-release.
- Hooks / layout (from the `setup.sh` and hook headers): auto-propagation of the post-update hook is guarded (commit reachable from the submodule's upstream default branch, not a downgrade) with opt-outs `git config constitution.autoPropagate false` and `CONSTITUTION_AUTO_PROPAGATE=off`; `setup.sh --allow-global-hooks-path`; shared `scripts/lib/hook_install.sh`; a submodule-side `post-checkout` consumer hook.
- `scripts/release_publish_gitea.sh`: publishes a release to the private `m3w` Gitea over its REST API (token from the environment only; refuses to publish an `[Unreleased]` section under a tag). Exercised only by its own help text in this documentation pass; no live publish is claimed.
- `scripts/constitution_ext/`: an extension engine (`validate`, `list`, `info`, `add`, `remove`, `enable`, `disable`, `verify`, `lock`, `doctor`, `catalog`, `wire`, `unwire`) with pinned installs, consent, signed local state and atomic journaled transactions. Experimental, dormant, not independently GO'd (rounds 10 and 11 fixes landed, round 11 fixes and the round 12 A3 staging-pid fix (c252eed) pending independent re-review). The `/constitution` command is implemented and `/constitution extensions ...` delegates to this engine; installer and post-update-hook wiring of extensions are UNCONFIRMED; per-agent shim formats other than Claude are UNCONFIRMED against live agents. Design: `docs/design/constitution_extensions_plan.md`.
- Documentation added: installer user guide, manual and FAQ, tutorials 01 to 08, a setup-prompt versioning guide, a release publishing guide, Mermaid diagrams (all linked from the README), quick start, troubleshooting guide, extensions guide / manual / FAQ / tutorial 07, decisions-wizard guide / FAQ / tutorial 08 (reconciled 2026-10-30 with the landed `scripts/decisions/constitution_decisions.sh`), and diagrams for architecture, update flow, trust model, extension lifecycle and decision flow.
- `docs/prompts/setup/latest.md` as the always-current versioned setup prompt, with follow-ups under `docs/prompts/setup/followups/` and `scripts/prompts/rotate_setup_prompt.sh` that rotates the old latest to `NN_NAME.md`.

### Changed

- `submodules/constitution` is the standard consumer layout; the legacy `./constitution` layout stays supported by every script. README install example updated accordingly.
- Layout-hardened hooks and scripts so both layouts work.
- README: latest-tag fact corrected (newest existing tag is `helixconstitution-v72.2`, checked with `git tag`); installer section placed at the top with one snippet per upstream and a link to the design plans.

### Changed — hub engine pins (update-all refresh, commit `a5cee41`)

- `claude_toolkit` `f24e091`, `containers` `22b4970`, `design-toolkit` `1b9abac`, `llmctl` `4fbef9e`, `open_design` `3805d03`, `verify` `cda25a0`; manifest re-pinned.

### Fixed

- Layout hardening so every script and hook works with both `submodules/constitution` and the legacy `./constitution` (setup, post-merge hook, evidence-store guard, canonical-root clarity gate and its mutation test, fast-cycle consumers, multitrack constitution sync, CLI-agent plugin installer, templates).
- Documentation drift: README, quick start, troubleshooting, manuals and tutorials re-checked flag by flag against `--help` of the installer, updater, decisions wizard, rotation script and Gitea publisher (no flag in the docs is absent from the scripts); the rotation script's dirty-tree refusal (exit 3 unless `--allow-dirty`) and the wizard's exit codes added to the troubleshooting guide; the orphaned extensions design plan linked from the README.

### Security

- Installer: never pushes; post-run no-push check; deny list for the Claude family; `--allow-unrestricted-agent` required for kimi, opencode, pi; sanitised git environment; credentials stripped from printed URLs; `--restore` path rules; refuses root without `--yes`; `curl | bash` runs a single `main()` invoked last, so a truncated download executes nothing. Trust model: [diagram](docs/diagrams/trust_model.md).
- Decisions wizard: a locked constitution rule can only be kept or tightened (weakening exits 4); secret-like values are refused; writes are confined to the project (and the user file with `--scope user`).
- Gitea publisher: token from the environment only, never argv, never printed, sent through a 0600 curl config; refuses to publish an `[Unreleased]` section under a tag.

### Known limits (§11.4.6)

- The installer's "agent never pushes" gate is a best-effort tripwire, not a boundary: it misses a push by URL to an unconfigured remote that updates no tracking ref, pushes to other repositories, and a push the remote reverses before the second look; a push by someone else during the run is a false positive. Whether `claude` enforces every form of the deny list was not checked live (UNCONFIRMED).
- Agent driving (claude family, provider aliases, kimi, opencode, pi) is covered only by unit-class tests with a fake agent binary (§11.4.289: a double is not live proof); no live LLM run was made for this documentation. Provider aliases, kimi, opencode and pi are UNCONFIRMED; opencode and pi end with exit 6 (success unverified).
- Lock reclaim differs between scripts: the installer reclaims a stale `.git/helix-constitution-install.lock` under a link-created `.reclaim` marker (only one of several runs replaces it), while `rotate_setup_prompt.sh` takes a stale `.rotate.lock` over by renaming its `pid` file away and re-checking the dead owner. Both refuse a non-regular lock; the two protocols are not shared code.
- Decisions wizard residuals (from `docs/design/decisions_wizard_plan.md`): a path-based copy after the last identity check remains a documented residual (note 6); `layer.mode=off` conflicts with §11.4.279(E) and is an open operator question; `bootstrap.sh` does not yet read `.constitution/decisions.yml` (the wizard prints the command and env names); the two wizard gates are not wired into the gate ledger, the pre-build sweep or the post-update hook; the enforce-eligible stems other than `gate` are UNCONFIRMED; review rounds for the wizard (tests `tests/decisions/test_round8.sh` to `test_round11.sh` exist) exceeded the §11.4.276 round budget (§11.4.276(G) makes 5 the default and hard ceiling; 6 or 7 only by a recorded operator decision naming the item) and continued under a documented overridable-budget decision (UNCONFIRMED as a recorded operator decision); an independent GO after round 11 was reported to the documentation pass, not re-verified here.
- Extensions engine: experimental, dormant, not independently GO'd (rounds 10 and 11 fixes landed, round 11 fixes and the round 12 A3 staging-pid fix (c252eed) pending independent re-review). The `/constitution` command is implemented (generated shims for claude, gemini, qwen, codex; opencode, kimi and pi are not wired); extension installer/post-update wiring and per-agent shim formats other than Claude are UNCONFIRMED against live agents.
- Extensions engine known limits, NOT fixed (no GO claimed): remove can delete a shared tree after `enable` re-consent in a second clone holding a copied lock (F-1); records and add are not bound to the constitution root (F-2); a parent-directory swap during the tree copy can copy outside content, caught afterwards by the digest check (S2); no reference counting across projects. The ownership redesign (marker set only by `add`, bound to root realpath and tree identity) is a tracked release-2 item. A3 (out-of-range pid in a staging directory name crashed every mutating command) is fixed. See `docs/design/constitution_extensions_plan.md`.
- Prefix installer `scripts/install_action_prefix.sh` (used by `install_cli_agent_plugins.sh`): the containment checks (settings directory resolves inside the project, hard-link count of `settings.json`, now a warning only) are check-then-write, so they are TOCTOU-narrowed, not closed; a local attacker who can swap a path component between check and use is not defended against (shell has no openat/O_NOFOLLOW chain). Fixed in this release (committed): `scripts/install_action_prefix.sh`, which `install_cli_agent_plugins.sh` runs, replaces `settings.json`, its `.bak` and the temporary file only through fresh `mktemp` inodes renamed into place (never written through); a `settings.json` with more than one hard link (for example after a `cp -al` snapshot) is NOT refused: it is replaced by a new file, the other links keep their old content, and a warning is printed; an existing `settings.json` keeps its mode (read with `stat -L`, so a symlink to a 0644 file yields 0644), a NEW `settings.json` or `.bak` gets at most 0644 (0666 & ~umask & ~022: umask 000 gives 644, 077 gives 600, 027 gives 640, 022 gives 644) and a NEW `.claude` directory is created without group/other write; when `settings.json` is a symlink it is replaced by a regular file (the link target keeps its old content) and a stderr warning naming it is printed; a `settings.json` that is a directory is refused early with a clear message (nothing written, no leftover seed file); anything other than exactly ONE JSON object (invalid JSON, empty or whitespace-only file, `null`, an array, several concatenated documents such as `{} {}`, or a malformed `hooks` / `UserPromptSubmit` structure) is REFUSED with a `REFUSED` message and rc 1, file unchanged, on both the jq and the python3 path, and the jq path verifies the merged result (one object, hook present) before the rename; and the generator is run with an ambient `HELIX_ACTION_OUT_ROOT` unset.
- `scripts/release_publish_gitea.sh` was exercised only through `--help` in this documentation pass; no live publish is claimed.
- Only Markdown documents are shipped (formats export is off).

## helixconstitution-v72.2 — 2026-10-30 — §11.4.291 capacity-aware decision-model placement, §11.4.230(F) continuous release train with the §11.4.276(G) five-round review cap, llmctl 3.4.0 pin

**Status of this entry: RELEASE v72.2** (tag `helixconstitution-v72.2`). Built on `a5cee41` (the 2026-10-29 update-all-submodules commit on top of v72.1, which also added the `upstreams/m3w.sh` upstream). Markdown-only documentation policy applies (no generated twins). The two entries below, dated 2026-10-28 and 2026-10-30, describe the anchor work in full; this entry lists what the release contains and what stays open.

### Added

- **§11.4.291 — Capacity-aware decision-model placement** (see the 2026-10-28 entry below): a local decision model runs only when the host's live load leaves room for it; otherwise decision traffic falls back, visibly and with a recorded reason, to the hosted endpoint (key present) or to a reasoned absence; on-demand `llmctl start` / `stop` only, never `enable`. Propagation gate `CM-COVENANT-114-291-PROPAGATION`; deferrals `OWED-GATE-165` to `OWED-GATE-168`. Independent reviews: rounds 2, 3 and 4 NO-GO, each fixed; round-4 follow-ups U1 and T2 recorded.
- **§11.4.230(F) — Continuous release train, and §11.4.276(G) — five-round review cap** (see the 2026-10-30 entry below), with dated amendment notes in §11.4.126, §11.4.134, §11.4.235(B)/(D) and the §11.4.288 Terms. Deferrals `OWED-GATE-169` to `OWED-GATE-173`; `CM-REVIEW-ROUND-BUDGET` (OWED-GATE-111) contract amended. Independent §11.4.209 review: rounds 1 and 2 NO-GO (fixed), round 3 GO.

### Pending operator decisions (release-train amendment; conservative defaults applied)

1. Whether an `important` `source-defect` may trigger an extension cycle — default **no** (it goes to §11.4.276(E)(3)).
2. The extension length `E_ext` — default **0** (no automatic extension).
3. The consumer `R_max` right — a consumer may declare `R_max` **below 5, never above** (6 or 7 only by a recorded operator decision naming the item).
4. `minor` / `nit` `source-defect` findings may ship as follow-ups — **applied**.
5. Open hardening may ride a release only in provisional-eligible classes (dev / pre-release); (L1), (L6) and (L7) entries **never** ride — **applied**.
6. The follow-up register location — default the **§11.4.93 store**, with `docs/FOLLOW_UPS_NEXT_PHASE.md` (or the consumer's equivalent) as a view of it.

### Changed — hub engine pin

- `llmctl` v3.4.0 (`4b68967`), fast-forward from `4fbef9e` (35 commits). `helix-deps.yaml` ref follows the gitlink. It is the first vendored llmctl that ships `llmctl capacity --json` (§11.4.291(C)); no placement verdict was computed on any host for this release. Checked: constitution scripts call `llmctl decide`, `llmctl models` and `llmctl require-validated` only; there is no `llmctl enable`, `disable`, `start`, `stop`, `switch` or `auto` call in `scripts/`, `skills/`, `plugins/`, `mcp/` or `actions/` (text search, `git grep`), so nothing needed changing for §11.4.291(G).

### Reconciliations made while assembling

- The (a) and (b) change sets were built on the same base (`eca3c33`); (b) contains (a), so the eight commits were applied in order onto `a5cee41` with no conflict (cherry-pick; the commit trailers were normalised to `Claude Sonnet 5.5`, since none of the commits had been pushed). Vocabulary was checked between the two: there is no `verdict_class` token anywhere in the repository; §11.4.291 uses `verdict` and `route` (`local` | `cloud` | `none`) as fields of its `placement` record, and §11.4.230(F) / §11.4.276(G) neither cite §11.4.291 nor use those field names; `GO-with-follow-ups` is spelled the same way in every occurrence. No text change was needed.

### Known imperfections (sorted by severity)

1. **MEDIUM** — §11.4.291: the last recorded independent review round (4) returned NO-GO (minor); its fixes landed in the round-4 commit and its two follow-ups were recorded, but no later round that returned GO is recorded in this repository (UNCONFIRMED).
2. **MEDIUM** — No placement verdict, `placement` record or lease registry exists on any host; all §11.4.291 and §11.4.230(F) / §11.4.276(G) mechanism gates are deferrals (`OWED-GATE-165` to `173`), not shipped code.
3. **LOW** — Release-train review minors NOT folded in (they need an independent review and a lockstep edit of the full §11.4.230 block in the four carriers): a release-boundary note in §11.4.235(E.5); the "strictly increasing" wording; the carried-count wording; a counter-range note; §11.4.276(G.2) cites §11.4.184 (SonarQube tooling) for "a security finding", a mis-citation. Tracked as follow-ups for the next iteration.
4. **LOW** — `upstreams/m3w.sh` (added in `4488f83`, not by this release) carries a copied comment saying GitHub is the primary upstream; its own `UPSTREAMABLE_PRIMARY=0` is correct. Text only.
5. **LOW** — No generated twins (Markdown-only base).
6. **MEDIUM** — Pre-existing full-suite failures, tracked as follow-ups for v72.3. The §11.4.40 run of this release: 179 of 220 entries exit 0; covenant gates 104/104 and mutations 101/101. The other 41 fail identically (same exit code, same failure text) at the pre-release base `a5cee41`; this release adds none. Real canon defects: `CM-KILLPG-PGID-GUARD` (an unguarded `os.killpg()` call); `CM-DANGEROUS-COMBINATION-FAIL-CLOSED` (an exception handler whose body is only `pass`); `CM-CLI-AGENT-PLUGINS-WIRED` (`skills/scheduled-work-queue/register.sh` is mode 100644, and `scripts/post_update_hook.sh` never calls `install_action_plugins`; its mutation test fails its golden-good copy for the same reason); `tests/test_cli_agent_plugins.sh` (an installed skill is a copy, not a symlink); `CM-ORACLE-STRATEGY-NAMED-AND-INDEPENDENT` (`scripts/anchors/tests/test_query_us2.py` lacks oracle-strategy annotations); `scripts/anchors/tests` (24 of 61 pytest cases fail once pytest and PyYAML are present). Not root-caused one by one: `CM-INSTRUMENT-TRAP-SCAN` (BLIND), `CM-CONTROL-NEEDLE-REQUIRED`, `CM-CONTINUUM-RESUME-ENGINE-PRESENT`, `CM-RULE-BINDS-TO-SEAM`, `CM-UNREFERENCED-GATE-BOUND-OR-RETIRED`, `CM-MUTATION-SCORE-ON-DIFF`, `CM-GIT-HOOKS-INSTALLED`, the `CM-OPENDESIGN-UI-SYSTEM` mutation test, `CM-CODEGRAPH-MCP-HEALTH` and its mutation test, and nine `tests/jev` scripts that appear to read the host toolkit MCP configuration (UNCONFIRMED). Gates that need a consumer layout or consumer data and fail in a standalone canon checkout: bridge-layer presence, canonical-root clarity, escape ratchet, dependency-register verdicts, mechanism-observed-refusing, the zero-findings sweep and ratchet, version-increment-on-deploy (SKIP), healthcheck ports, first-refusal, the two Jev detection and token-reduction gates, nested-markdown-fence leak. The host has no pytest; it was run through `uv run --with pytest`.

## Unreleased — 2026-10-30 — §11.4.230(F) continuous release train and §11.4.276(G) five-round review cap with minor findings carried to the next iteration

**Status of this entry: released in `helixconstitution-v72.2`** (see that entry above). Rounds 1 and 2 of the independent §11.4.209 review returned NO-GO; their fixes are listed under "Review round 1 fixes" and "Review round 2 fixes" below; round 3 returned GO.

### Added

- **§11.4.230(F) — Continuous release train.** Operator mandate of 2026-10-30 quoted verbatim (typos preserved) in §11.4.230(F) and, since review round 1, in §11.4.276(G). Closed release states `candidate` / `hardening-in-background` / `closed-out` and the NEXT-CANDIDATE; QA builds carry pre-release ids inside the candidate (`-dev-` decimal carry) and the release promotes the last QA-built artifact; release N ships as soon as its seam holds and the declared release trigger fires (default `per-ready-candidate`); the release seam it never relaxes (§11.4.40, §11.4.185, §11.4.236, §11.4.129, §11.4.264, (E.2), §11.4.290(F)/(G)); a release may ship with open hardening only in a provisional-eligible class, a production-class release needs `production-ready`; the handover in the same write (next id minted, open ledger entries carried under their ids, background streams continue, findings against N land in N+1, N never patched or re-tagged); the loop does not stop at a release; release interval, handover gap and carried debt as trend metrics, never gates; four failure modes with detectors; an operator-readable worked example of the loop end to end.
- **§11.4.276(G) — Review-round cap amendment.** `R_max` default and hard ceiling 5 (6 or 7 only by a recorded operator decision naming the item); minor-class findings end the loop with a `GO-with-follow-ups` verdict and become tracked follow-up items in the §11.4.93 store for the next iteration, with a fresh budget; a closed severity vocabulary {`critical`, `important`, `minor`, `nit`} recorded by the reviewer; the extension length `E_ext` is consumer DATA by recorded operator decision, default 0 (no automatic extension), and when declared runs at most one cycle only for a `critical` `source-defect` or a broken proof-of-batch stack; then (E)(3). The mandate is quoted verbatim in (G) itself (the §11.4.267(E) form). Precedence note: (G) amends (A), (E)(3) and (F).
- **Gates:** five recommended gates registered as deferrals `OWED-GATE-169` to `OWED-GATE-173` (`CM-RELEASE-TRAIN-HANDOVER-NO-GAP`, `CM-RELEASE-LEDGER-CARRIED-FORWARD`, `CM-RELEASE-NOT-WITHHELD-AND-CLASS-HONEST`, `CM-REVIEW-EXTENSION-CYCLE-JUSTIFIED`, `CM-REVIEW-FOLLOW-UPS-REGISTERED`), documented in `docs/owed_gate_implementations.md` (revision 13); the `CM-REVIEW-ROUND-BUDGET` (OWED-GATE-111) contract amended to match (G). Gate code is not claimed shipped.

### Changed

- **§11.4.126:** a dated amendment note: terminal condition (A) ends the scope of release N only, and the loop continues on N+1.
- **Review round 1 fixes (2026-10-30):** S1 the phantom citation `§11.4.290(5)` corrected to §11.4.290(F) labelling and (G) release and changelog duty (three places, canon and four carriers); S2 version ids reconciled with §11.4.235(B) (pre-release `-dev-` ids inside the candidate, one increment at the release instant, release promotes the last QA-built artifact per §11.4.264, worked example corrected, dated precedence note in §11.4.235(B)); S3 dated in-place amendment notes in §11.4.134, §11.4.235(D) and the §11.4.288 Terms, each quoting the mandate; S4 (E.3) (L6) and (L7) entries never ride a release of any class; S5 the mandate quoted verbatim in §11.4.276(G), the extension length no longer a literal; P1 closed severity set defined as a new rule in (G.2) (the false claim that §11.4.235(D) covers severity removed); P2 the broken-gate trigger limited to proof-of-batch gates; P3 the state `released` renamed `closed-out`. Pending operator decisions (conservative defaults applied): (1) whether an `important` `source-defect` may trigger an extension (default: no, it goes to (E)(3)); (2) the extension length (default 0); (3) removing the consumer-wide `R_max` 6-7 right (kept removed); (4) letting `minor`/`nit` `source-defect` findings ship as follow-ups (applied); (5) open hardening only in provisional-eligible classes (applied); (6) the follow-up register location per project (default the §11.4.93 store; a consumer register file is a view of it). **Review round 2 fixes (2026-10-30):** I-1 the `-dev-` counter never resets across a release boundary (§11.4.151), every increment including the release-boundary one is an operation of the one version tool, (F.4)(1), (F.7)(4) and (F.9) aligned (`<PREFIX>-1.0.9-dev-0.0.3` then `<PREFIX>-1.1.0-dev-0.0.4`); M-1 (L1) entries (a live-FAIL reopen) excluded like (L6)/(L7), OWED-GATE-171 aligned; M-2 (F.4)(5) follow-ups included in the (F.1) state definitions; N-1 severity scoped to `source-defect`, `critical`/`important` never minor-class; N-2 a consumer may declare `R_max` below 5, never above; N-3 examples use `<PREFIX>-1.x.y`. Revisions after the round-2 fixes: `Constitution.md` 87, `CLAUDE.md` 90, `AGENTS.md` 87, `QWEN.md` 84, `GEMINI.md` 58, `docs/owed_gate_implementations.md` 15; generated artifacts regenerated.
- **Carriers:** the full §11.4.230 block (heading and new clause (F), process note now naming (G) as next free letter) is identical in `CLAUDE.md` (revision 88), `AGENTS.md` (85), `QWEN.md` (82), `GEMINI.md` (56); the compact §11.4.276 entry gains one amendment sentence; `Constitution.md` revision 85. The `Status summary` cells were not touched.
- **Generated:** `constitution_index.yaml`, `groups/*.md`, `progressive/catalog.json`, `progressive/rules/11.4.126.md`, `11.4.230.md`, `11.4.276.md` and `progressive/rules_manifest.json` regenerated from `Constitution.md`. README not updated (facts and badge row not re-measured in this change).

## Unreleased — 2026-10-28 — §11.4.291 capacity-aware decision-model placement (local only when it fits, visible fallback to hosted, on-demand llmctl, never `enable`)

**Status of this entry: released in `helixconstitution-v72.2`** (see that entry above). Independent §11.4.209 review rounds 2, 3 and 4 are recorded below; the last recorded round (4) returned NO-GO (minor) and was fixed.

### Added

- **§11.4.291 — Capacity-aware decision-model placement.** Operator mandate of 2026-10-28 (quoted verbatim in the anchor) plus two same-day mandates relayed by the conductor (llmctl models keep a host reserve; the constitution starts and stops llmctl on demand and never runs `enable`). Closed per-profile verdict in llmctl's own names `fits` / `fits_with_reduced_threads` / `does_not_fit`, plus a consumer-derived `undetermined` (never read as fits; liftable for a start only by a §11.4.271 waiver or a recorded `override: "<id>"`, never past `does_not_fit` or llmctl's pressure gate); a contract table for `llmctl capacity --json` (schema `llmctl.capacity/1`, required fields, exits 0 / 75 / 3, the rules that yield `undetermined`); a host reserve that composes §12.6, §12.12, §11.4.225 and the unrelaxed §11.4.277(E)(3) start gate, with no new number; a live input contract (MemAvailable, PSI, load, cgroup limits, running and candidate footprints with provenance, RLIMIT_NPROC headroom), canonical source `llmctl capacity --json` (llmctl 3.4.0, separate work, UNCONFIRMED until vendored); re-evaluation including degradation of a running model (route switches first, a bounded drain, then `llmctl stop` by the last lease holder; the constitution stops only what it started); a per-user, host-level lease set for profiles shared by several sessions or projects, and a fallback with reason `endpoint_gone` when an endpoint disappears; fallback route `cloud` when a hosted key is present by variable NAME (hosted never enforces, so enforced types return to shadow) or `none` with a reasoned absence; a visible notice and a `placement` record in `jev-detection.json` for every transition; on-demand lifecycle only (never `enable` / `disable`, never `switch` or an evicting `auto`, every llmctl call time-bounded).
- **Refinement notes** (dated 2026-10-28) at the end of the §11.4.279, §11.4.282 and §11.4.284 blocks, each pointing at §11.4.291 without restating it.
- **Gates:** `CM-COVENANT-114-291-PROPAGATION` (generated by `scripts/gates/covenant_propagation_wrappers_generate.sh` from a new data-pack row, with its generated mutation test; seam row in `rule_seam_bindings.tsv`). Four recommended gates registered as deferrals `OWED-GATE-165` to `OWED-GATE-168` (`CM-DECISION-PLACEMENT-VERDICT-RECORDED`, `CM-DECISION-FALLBACK-ON-NO-FIT`, `CM-CONSTITUTION-NEVER-ENABLES-LLMCTL`, `CM-DECISION-MODEL-DEGRADATION-REEVALUATED`), documented in `docs/owed_gate_implementations.md` (revision 12).

### Changed

- **Carriers:** identical compact §11.4.291 entry appended at the end of `CLAUDE.md` (revision 87), `AGENTS.md` (84), `QWEN.md` (81), `GEMINI.md` (55); `Constitution.md` revision 84. The `Status summary` cells were not touched (new amendments are recorded here, per the archive policy).
- **Anchor machinery:** `scripts/anchors/anchor_lib.py` maps §11.4.291 to group `host-and-resource-safety`; `scripts/anchors/tests/test_query_us2.py` universal count 188 to 189; `constitution_index.yaml`, `groups/*.md`, `progressive/catalog.json`, `progressive/rules/*` and `progressive/rules_manifest.json` regenerated (index 301 anchors across 12 groups; progressive split 302 anchors).
- **Docs:** `docs/jev/governance-guide.md` (revision 3, new section "Capacity-aware placement") and `docs/jev/faq.md` (revision 2, question 23) explain placement, fallback and the on-demand rule; README revision 15 (facts, badge row, anchor and release tables re-measured; the release table now lists `helixconstitution-v72.1`, which already carried §11.4.290).

### Fixed — review round 2 (independent Opus review, NO-GO, 5 source-defects)

- Verdict and route names now match llmctl 3.4.0: `fits_with_reduced_threads` (was `fits_reduced`), routes `local` / `cloud` / `none` (was `hosted`); `undetermined` is stated as consumer-derived, and §11.4.291(C) carries a contract table for `llmctl capacity --json` (required fields, exit statuses, the derivation rules).
- New §11.4.291(A.1): the `undetermined` interim can be lifted only per host and profile by a §11.4.271 waiver or a recorded `override: "<id>"`; the §11.4.277(E)(3) pressure gate still applies; routing to an already-running operator-started instance under `undetermined` is allowed, recorded and announced.
- New §11.4.291(J): a per-user, host-level lease set per profile (stop only on the last lease, dead holders reaped, operator-started models never stopped) and the `endpoint_gone` fallback.
- §11.4.291(D)(1): a bounded drain before `llmctl stop`; the "no decision is dropped" claim softened to "no decision silently lost".
- The llmctl 3.3.2 `llmctl decide capacity --json` description corrected: partly live (RAM and VRAM budgets), but no PSI, CPU load, cgroup limit or per-profile verdict.
- Recorded, not fixed: the review found that `cm_gate_ledger_ratchet.sh` accepts duplicate deferral IDs (its reviewer mutant M4 survived); written up as a follow-up in `docs/owed_gate_implementations.md`, no gate name minted.

### Fixed — review round 4 (independent Opus review of 2e647ae, NO-GO minor)

- §11.4.291(J)(3): a `stopping` mark carries the stopper's pid or heartbeat and a bounded stop timeout; when the stopper is dead or the timeout passed, the mark is cleared under the lock (unit still running: set returns to normal, model not stopped; unit gone: set removed; recorded either way). OWED-GATE-168 gains the golden case "stopper killed mid-stop: mark cleared, not stuck".
- §11.4.291(J)(1): a heartbeat TTL MUST exceed the re-evaluation cadence plus the llmctl call timeout.
- Ratchet follow-up in `docs/owed_gate_implementations.md`: reviewer mutant P4 (swapping two deferral ids, 165 and 166) added beside M4 and N3.

### Fixed — review round 3 (independent Opus review of 7cd7550, NO-GO, 4 source-defects)

- §11.4.291(E): when no candidate of the session fits, `fallback.recommended: local` is untrusted (llmctl `lib/capacity.sh` line 193 answers `local` when another decision profile fits or any decide unit runs, `degraded` included); the route is `cloud` or `none` from the consumer's own §11.4.279(E) key-by-name check, never a `degraded` unit. Contract table and OWED-GATE-166 updated.
- §11.4.291(J): a lease-lock timeout means no lease (no local route, no start, fallback `lease_lock_timeout`); the last-lease stop is atomic with acquisition (set marked `stopping` under the lock, acquisition on it refused); the holder is the long-lived session process or a session heartbeat with a TTL, never a short-lived hook, bootstrap or MCP-call pid; a reap alone never stops the model. OWED-GATE-166 and 168 updated.
- Wording: exit 0 means "fits or already running"; the `host` row names its real keys; vendoring llmctl 3.4.0 alone does not end `undetermined` for a start; the ratchet follow-up now also covers renumbered ids (mutant N3).
- Noted in the governance guide (llmctl side, not fixed here): llmctl's `running` detection matches exact profile names, so instance-suffixed units such as `decide.1` are missed.

### Honest boundaries

- Nothing is measured: no placement verdict has been computed on any host, no producer of the `placement` record exists, and the vendored llmctl (3.3.2, `11a9154`) has no `llmctl capacity`; its `llmctl decide capacity --json` is partly live (RAM budget = min(live `MemAvailable` - 4 GiB, 60% of `MemTotal` - running reservations), live free VRAM, per `lib/catalog.sh` lines 1-12 and 365-400) but has no PSI, CPU load, cgroup limit or per-profile verdict, and is not accepted as a live verdict on its own. The `llmctl capacity --json` contract was read from an uncommitted llmctl 3.4.0 working tree and one run of it on one host (`schema` `llmctl.capacity/1`, exit 0); it is not vendored.
- The anchor reads `undetermined` as "do not start a local model", so until a live capacity source exists no constitution automation starts a local decision model on any host. Whether the operator wants that interim effect is an open question, not an operator decision.
- No constitution script is changed by this entry; whether any existing script already conflicts with clause (G) was checked only by a text search (no `llmctl enable`, `start`, `switch` or `auto` call found in `scripts/jev`, `skills/jev-decision-layer` or `scripts/post_update_hook.sh`), which is a lead, not the owed gate.

## helixconstitution-v72.1 — 2026-10-28 — credscan, hub engine pins, propagation-family fixes

**Status of this entry: RELEASE v72.1** (tag `helixconstitution-v72.1`). Built on `511b3a4` (the landing after v72); Markdown-only documentation policy applies (no generated twins).

### Added

- **credscan** (`scripts/credscan/`): a counts-only credential-shape scanner with a baseline ratchet, a `credscan_hook.py` entry point and a low-priority systemd check timer (user scope). It prints counts, never values (§11.4.10). 67 tests (`scripts/credscan/tests/test_credscan.py`). Guide, manual and FAQ under `docs/`. The hook is NOT installed into any `settings.json` by this release; installation stays an operator act.
- Tests for the fixes below: `covenant_propagation_default_root_test.sh`, `covenant_propagation_suite_default_root_test.sh`, `covenant_propagation_superspec_exclusion_test.sh`, `covenant_propagation_third_party_hub_exclusion_test.sh` (all under `scripts/gates/`).

### Changed — hub engine pins (verified by `git ls-remote` on the real remotes)

- `llmctl` v3.3.2 (`11a9154`), `claude_toolkit` v1.33.2 (`36bdfd9`), `continuum` continuum-0.1.2 (`9d95651`), `containers` containers-2.0.0 (`bfd0e98`, Go module path stays `digital.vasic.containers`). `helix-deps.yaml` refs follow the gitlinks (`CM-HELIX-DEPS-REF-MATCHES-GITLINK` PASS, `CM-GITLINK-ON-REMOTE` PASS).

### Fixed

- The propagation family's default root is the repository root, not a cwd-relative parent (the gates previously audited a wrong carrier set once the submodules were populated).
- Carrier exclusions for the vendored third-party submodules (agentic-validation, claude-video, donespec, repo-proof, skill-doctor, verfix, verify, watch-skill) and for the third-party `open_design` and `verification` checkouts, and for the SpecKit sample-project fixtures (llmctl, claude_toolkit, hub superspec), each with its justification in `config/covenant_propagation_exclusions.tsv`.
- `docs/jev/user-manual.md` no longer says that no `decide-*` profile has ever been run: on one host (2026-10-28) `decide-nli`, `decide-laya`, `decide-kev-08b` were reported `pass`, `decide-julia` `fail`, `decide-tiny` `undetermined` (UNCONFIRMED lead: no committed evidence artifact). Enforcement is still not claimed live. No anchor text (§11.4.277, §11.4.279) contained the stale claim in this base; the anchors are unchanged.

### Known imperfections (sorted by severity)

1. **MEDIUM** — The `decide-*` validation results are an UNCONFIRMED lead (operator-relayed, one host, 2026-10-28; no committed evidence artifact); the llmctl release that produced it is UNCONFIRMED. Enforcement by decision models stays unclaimed (§11.4.279(D)).
2. **MEDIUM** — The propagation family audits every checked-out `CLAUDE/AGENTS/QWEN/GEMINI.md`, so each new third-party submodule needs a justified row in `config/covenant_propagation_exclusions.tsv` or the suite goes red once submodules are populated (found twice in this release: `open_design`, `verification`). A gate that discovers third-party carriers by `third_party: true` in `helix-deps.yaml` instead of a hand list is owed.
3. **MEDIUM** — Review: round 1 (Opus, independent) returned GO with 2 IMPORTANT + 13 MINOR; the 2 IMPORTANT and minors 4, 5, 7, 8, 9, 10 were fixed afterwards and verified by the author's own reruns (gates 103/103), but NO second independent review round was run on those fixes.
4. **LOW** — Round-1 minors left open: credscan baseline write is not O_NOFOLLOW (symlink/umask window); the PLACEHOLDER regex skips tokens containing test/sample/fake/example; `default_roots()` hardcodes `~/.claude-shared`; exclusion globs start with an unanchored `*`; credscan manual says twins are stale while the base is Markdown-only; user guide mislabels engine exit codes 1-4 as wrapper-only; the hook default mode (block, fail-open) is not documented prominently; docs disagree on whether `JEV_GATE=off` records its use (canon §11.4.279(F) says it logs nothing, the user manual says gate-off.log).
5. **LOW** — The weekly llmctl revalidate timer from the operator's user-scope kit is NOT shipped: it is host-specific (user systemd units, host paths), not hub-appropriate. Owed as a consumer-side item.
6. **LOW** — No generated twins (html/pdf/docx): this base is Markdown-only (§11.4.65 export off), so none were regenerated.
7. **LOW** — credscan is shipped but its hook is NOT installed into any `settings.json`, and the timer is not enabled; both are operator acts. Its baseline ratchet starts from the first run on each host.
8. **LOW** — `post_update_hook.sh` run against a consumer root without `helix-deps.yaml` fails by design (§11.4.31); run it with `PROJECT_ROOT` set.
9. **TRIVIAL** — The first covenant-suite run in the release work was invalidated by an external `timeout` kill (carrier tempfile removed) and repeated unkilled; the recorded counts are from the unkilled runs.

## Unreleased — 2026-10-28 (second) — landing change set on top of v72: Markdown-only amendment (formats_export OFF), dispatch-tier guard, v71/v72 merge, claude_toolkit re-pin

**Status of this entry: UNRELEASED.** No tag exists for it; the newest tag is `helixconstitution-v72`. It records, in one place, every change in the range `origin/main..HEAD` that earlier entries did not describe. Items already described by the entries below (the §11.4.290 anchor, the review-round-1 fixes) are only cross-referenced here.

### Changed (canon, universal) — Markdown-only documentation, format exports OFF by default (operator order 2026-10-26, call of 2026-10-23 task 2)

- **Amended anchors:** §11.4.65 (document-export mandate; the `.md` is the single source of truth, the `.html` / `.pdf` / `.docx` legs bind ONLY while the switch is explicitly on), §11.4.60 (the sync covenant: "in sync across markdown + HTML + PDF" becomes "the `.md` is the single source of truth and is in sync with the project's state"), §11.4.75 (the bound doc classes are a triple only while the switch is on), §11.4.141 M7 ("Agents work only with Markdown; document exports are opt-in", PDF only on request by one `pandoc` command, DOCX never) and the §5 reference text. The verbatim 2026-05-19 mandate and its protections are kept as the forensic record and bind only while `formats_export=true`. Mirrors (`CLAUDE.md`, `AGENTS.md`, `GEMINI.md`, `QWEN.md`, `CLAUDE_ANCHORS_FULL.md`), `groups/`, `progressive/` and the index were regenerated.
- **The switch:** new `config/formats_export.conf` (`formats_export=false`, the only default) read by ONE helper, `scripts/formats_export_lib.sh` (`formats_export_enabled`, `formats_export_resolve`, `formats_export_skip_notice`, `formats_export_gate`). Resolution order: env `DOCS_EXPORT` (1/true/yes/on or 0/false/no/off, one run) > a repository policy file `docs/context/doc_export.policy` with the line `enabled=1` (a repository can only opt IN) > the conf default > fail-closed (unreadable or unparsable means off). Every consumer announces its skip (§11.4.106(E)), never silent.
- **Consumers wired:** `scripts/badges/compute_badges.sh` (Twins badge is GRAY "markdown only" while off; new `scripts/badges/test_twins_badge_formats_switch.sh`), `scripts/render/render-governance-twins.sh`, `scripts/translation/render-articles.sh`, `scripts/charts-engine/lib/export.py`, `scripts/catalog-engine/generate_catalog.py`, and the workable-items exporter (`export` and `subtask-export` honour the switch; `--formats` forces on, `--no-formats` forces off; new `formats_switch.go` with tests).
- **How a consumer opts back in:** `DOCS_EXPORT=1 <command>` for one run, or `enabled=1` in `docs/context/doc_export.policy`, or change the conf value (a constitution-level decision). Nothing is ever implicit.
- **Deletions:** 891 generated `.html` / `.pdf` / `.docx` twins of tracked Markdown were removed from this repository (the whole commit removes at least 8,712 such exports, counted as 7,821 in the parent plus 891 here; the owned-submodule share was not counted, so any larger total is UNCONFIRMED; hardlink backups and manifests were kept by that operation). No `.md` document was removed. `docs/BADGES.md` and `README.md` were updated to the markdown-only reading.
- **Nested pins moved by the same operation:** `docs_chain`, `token_optimizer` and `session_orchestrator` pointed at local twin-deletion commits. Those local commits are not published; the merge to v72 took v72's published pins (see below). The twin deletion is therefore applied here but not inside those three nested repositories.
- **Single-track ruling and test refresh in the same commit:** `scripts/hooks/test_guard_work_track_binding.sh` reflects the single-track ruling (the test adds a separate `unified-product` single-track row; `mistiq-vader` stays feature-bound as test data); `scripts/fastcycle/tests/test_render_keys_red.sh` adapted to the switch.

### Added — dispatch-tier guard (operator order 2026-10-26, "no heavy agent on a simple task")

- `scripts/fastcycle/tokens/dispatch_stamp.sh` now enforces an OPTIONAL `class=<name>` declared in a dispatch description: the §11.4.182 label's model and effort (and an explicit `.tool_input.model`) may not exceed the class caps, else the dispatch is BLOCKED (exit 2). Caps are data: `FC_DISPATCH_TIER_POLICY` (a TSV path) > `$CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR/docs/context/dispatch_tier_policy.tsv` > the built-in table (simple sonnet/medium, mechanical sonnet/high, standard sonnet/xhigh, heavy opus/max). A dispatch with NO `class=` is never refused by this check; an unknown class, an unreadable policy file or a row with an unknown cap name is refused (fail closed); an honest `?` label is allowed. This is a behaviour change for any caller that already put `class=` in a description. Proof: `scripts/fastcycle/tokens/test_dispatch_stamp_class.sh` (positive and refusal matrix, paired mutants).

### Changed — merges and landing of the earlier local work

- **v71 merge (Jev decision layer §11.4.279 to §11.4.286, label-sync, dependency submodules) completed** and **v72 (§11.4.287, §11.4.288, §11.4.289) merged on top**. The local optimistic-done anchor was drafted as §11.4.287 and is landed as **§11.4.290** (renumbered because v72 owns 287 to 289; see the §11.4.290 entry below). v72's own `cm_covenant_114_287_propagation*` gate files are untouched; ours are named 290.
- **Status-summary cells moved out of the always-loaded preamble:** the `Status summary` cell of `CLAUDE.md`, `AGENTS.md`, `QWEN.md`, `GEMINI.md` and `Constitution.md` is a one-line hook with a pointer; the history is kept verbatim, newest first, in `docs/amendment-log/status-summary-*.md` by the new `scripts/amendment_log/status_summary_archive.py` (`archive`, `verify`; guide `docs/scripts/status_summary_archive.md`, test `scripts/amendment_log/tests/test_status_summary_archive.sh`, 39 checks). After the v72 merge the archives were rebuilt from the full text (v72 had re-grown the cells and edited two archived entries) and verified.
- **CM-GATE-LEDGER-RATCHET** reads the archive named by the preamble as part of its corpus and reads `unimplemented=394 baseline=394`; the baseline was never raised.
- **CodeGraph hardening:** new `scripts/codegraph/codegraph_launch_class.py` (what an MCP entry really launches), `scripts/codegraph/codegraph_mcp_health.py` and gate `CM-CODEGRAPH-MCP-HEALTH`, hardened `codegraph_mcp_preflight.sh` and `runner_patches/mcpro1.py`, with tests and guides under `docs/scripts/`.
- **catalog-engine:** `scripts/catalog-engine/lib/parse_gate.py` recognises the three gate-registration forms and is shared with the pairing audit (guide `docs/scripts/catalog_engine_parse_gate.md`, tests under `scripts/catalog-engine/tests/`).
- **workable-items:** `diary.go` and `export.go` changes and the rebuilt tracked binaries (`bin/workable-items`, `bin/workable-items-linux`, byte-identical). `bin/.source.sha256` was NOT re-recorded (the gate that reads it is UNCONFIRMED, see the review report).

### Changed — nested pins (review round 1 and 2)

- `claude_toolkit`, `docs_chain` and `token_optimizer` are pinned to published commits only (a new gate, `CM-GITLINK-ON-REMOTE`, fails when a gitlink names a commit on no remote; see the entry below). `claude_toolkit` is re-pinned to `5d1c6eb` (tag `v1.33.1`, main tip of all four declared upstreams), which carries the §11.4.35 `## INHERITED FROM constitution/CLAUDE.md` pointer sections in its four carriers; `helix-deps.yaml` and the gitlink move together (`CM-HELIX-DEPS-REF-MATCHES-GITLINK`). `llmctl` and `session_orchestrator` take v72's published pins.

### Fixed — review round 2

- `CM-GITLINK-ON-REMOTE` no longer accepts a local unpushed tag, a local branch or the refs of a foreign remote as proof of publication: offline containment counts only `refs/remotes/<R>/*` of a remote whose URL equals the `.gitmodules` URL; `--online` re-validates against `git ls-remote --heads --tags` and is never an upgrade-on-any-output; new `--rev <commit>` judges the commit being pushed instead of the index. The paired mutation test grew from 10 to 17 checks and kills the two mutants the review found (local branches counted; `--online` upgrading on any output).
- `CM-CODEGRAPH-MCP-HEALTH` reports its documented SKIP (exit 3) in a checkout with nothing declared and nothing indexed: "indexed" now means `.codegraph/codegraph.db` exists, not that a `.codegraph/` directory does (the constitution tracks `.codegraph/.gitignore`, so the bare directory exists everywhere and the gate FAILed). Mutation-test cases added for the gitignore-only directory and for an indexed, undeclared project.
- Review-round-3 follow-ups (LOCAL, not yet reviewed or pushed): `config/covenant_propagation_exclusions.tsv` generalises the SpecKit-fixture row to `*/.specify/extensions/superspec/examples/*` (claude_toolkit vendors the same fixture as llmctl); `CM-GITLINK-ON-REMOTE` no longer counts refs of a foreign remote named `<remote>/<x>` offline (mutation case 13) and reports BLIND for `--online` in a shallow nested repo whose history was cut (mutation case 14); a tag-only publication stays UNPUBLISHED offline with a corrected message (a hard BLIND would contradict mutation case 8), `--online` is the documented pre-push mode.
- `scripts/fastcycle/tokens/test_dispatch_stamp_class.sh` pins `FC_DISPATCH_ITEM_ID_RE` in the three cases that did not (policy-as-data, project policy file, `--extract-item-id`), so it passes in a standalone checkout (33 passed, 0 failed, 1 announced SKIP because the consumer policy file is absent; 34/34 inside the consumer; it failed 2 of 33 outside the ATMOSphere consumer, and the "policy" cases could pass for the wrong reason because an item-prefix refusal also exits 2).

### Honest boundaries

- The unpublished intermediate commits of this range recorded nested pins that are on no remote (round-2 finding R2-F4); the final tip records only published pins. Publishing the three nested twin-deletion commits as tags is an operator decision.
- Failures that exist at v72 in the same environment (evidence files, the hooks map, oracle annotations) were not changed by this range: 330 of the 331 gate scripts shared with v72 return the same exit code at the tip (measured at 511b3a4); the one that differs is the timing-dependent `test_render.sh` G10b/G10c flake, which reproduces at v72.

## Unreleased — 2026-10-27 — §11.4.290 optimistic done with parallel background verification (PROVISIONAL-DONE vs VERIFIED-DONE)

**Status of this entry: UNRELEASED.** No tag exists for it; the newest tag is `helixconstitution-v72`. This entry is the local landing on top of the v72 merge: the anchor was drafted as 11.4.287 and renumbered to **11.4.290** because v72 owns §11.4.287 to §11.4.289 (the same gate files that v72 ships under the 287 name are untouched; ours are named 290). Nothing here is tagged or pushed by this change set. Until a tag exists the README version badge reads "unreleased" (amber) by construction, because `scripts/badges/compute_badges.sh` derives it from the first heading of this file.

### Added

- **§11.4.290 — Optimistic done with parallel background verification.** Operator mandate of 2026-10-27 (quoted verbatim in the anchor): mark things done and release as soon as possible, but validate and verify against machine evidence always, in parallel, in the background; reopen on contrary evidence; until proved not done, consider it done. Two labelled states: PROVISIONAL-DONE (implemented and wired per §11.4.197, background verification job scheduled; counts as done for planning, roadmap and release scope, never called verified, fixed or passing) and VERIFIED-DONE (the §11.4.108 runtime signature on a clean target at the §11.4.226 evidence-class floor; the only state those words may describe). The Status closed set of §11.4.15/§11.4.33 is NOT extended: the provisional state rides on `Ready for testing` / `In testing` plus a required `**Verification:** provisional — …` line, because the workable-items schema CHECK rejects any other Status literal and `parse.go` would silently demote an unknown one to `Queued`. Clauses (A)–(I): scheduling at the moment of marking, automatic non-silent reopen on contrary evidence (a RED on a pre-fix artifact is not contrary evidence), overdue handling, "V verified + P provisional (O overdue)" labelling, and the four-part release changelog duty (VERIFIED / PROVISIONAL / REOPENED / OVERDUE). It relaxes no verdict-absence seam (§11.4.115(F), §11.4.135, §11.4.236; only §11.4.236(4) or a §11.4.271 waiver does).
- **Refinement notes** (dated 2026-10-27) at the end of the §11.4.15, §11.4.33, §11.4.108, §11.4.123 and §11.4.226 blocks, each pointing at §11.4.290 without restating it.
- **Gates:** `CM-COVENANT-114-290-PROPAGATION` (generated by `scripts/gates/covenant_propagation_wrappers_generate.sh` from a new data-pack row, with its generated mutation test) and `CM-PROVISIONAL-DONE-ANCHOR-WIRED` (`scripts/gates/cm_provisional_done_anchor_wired.sh`, 117 checks: single canonical heading and block-start, 22 required markers, 6 escape-hatch flags, cross-references to the done/closure anchors and back-pointers from five of them, four carrier mirrors, skill, index, group file, rule file, catalog, data-pack row, gate files, hook binding) with its paired mutation test `cm_provisional_done_anchor_wired_mutation_test.sh` (117 RED and 3 BLIND cases, 1 control and 4 golden-FALSE GREEN cases).
- **Hook binding:** `scripts/post_update_hook.sh` step 5b now also runs `CM-PROVISIONAL-DONE-ANCHOR-WIRED` against the constitution root; `tests/test_post_update_hub_gates.sh` was extended to four stub gates (cases PDONE-FAIL, PDONE-BLIND, PDONE-SKIP added; 9 cases, GREEN, and RED against the unmodified hook).
- **Skill `skills/workable-item-lifecycle/`** version 1.1.0: new section "Provisional-done: optimistic done with background verification (§11.4.290)" with the state table, the `Verification:` line format, the auto-reopen rule, what does not reopen, overdue handling, labelling and the release changelog duty.

### Changed

- **Carriers:** `CLAUDE.md` revision 84, `AGENTS.md` 81, `QWEN.md` 78, `GEMINI.md` 52 (79 and 53 after the preamble move below; identical compact §11.4.290 entry appended at end of file in each; the §11.4.286 block is byte-unchanged). `Constitution.md` revision 81. Last-modified fields were set to a timestamp of the same length so the three preamble byte ratchets did not move.
- **Anchor machinery:** `scripts/anchors/anchor_lib.py` maps §11.4.290 to group `workable-items-and-tracking`; `scripts/anchors/tests/test_query_us2.py` universal count 184 to 185 (total 297); `constitution_index.yaml`, `groups/*.md`, `progressive/catalog.json`, `progressive/rules/*` and `progressive/rules_manifest.json` regenerated (index 297 anchors across 12 groups; progressive split 298 anchors, 300 artifacts).
- **README** revision 11: new section "Optimistic done with background verification (§11.4.290)" with a state diagram; the facts table, group table and badge row were re-measured. Several figures in the previous revision were stale (gates, mutation tests, Twins and Readiness badges, group counts) and are now the measured values; the own-org / third-party submodule split was not re-measured and is flagged as such.

### Fixed

- **Constitution preambles back under their byte ratchets: the `Status summary` amendment log moved out of the always-loaded preamble (ATM-1380, 2026-10-27).** `scripts/context_footprint.sh --check-budget` in the parent repository failed with 3 violations (`CLAUDE.md` preamble 140994 bytes vs ratchet 140000, `AGENTS.md` 133604 vs 133300, `Constitution.md` 158914 vs 157600; the preamble is everything before the first `## ` heading, which every `head`, nested-memory load or quick read pays). Cause, measured by bisecting this repository's history (preamble bytes CLAUDE / AGENTS / Constitution): 139422 / 132728 / 157017 at the merge-base `94b8836`; 139602 / 132908 / 157197 at the local Markdown-only amendment `a24b762` (+180 each); 140807 / 133417 / 158727 at the upstream v71 tip `0b94dfc`, already above the ratchets; 140994 / 133604 / 158914 in the staged merge (v71 tip + the 187-byte "Merge 2026-10-26" sentence). All of the growth, and 98 percent or more of the preamble (98.4, 98.4 and 98.1 percent), is the single `| Status summary | ... |` row (138752 / 131438 / 155887 bytes): an append-only log of the amendment rounds, although §11.4.61(A) defines the cell as a one-line current-state hook. The ratchets had been set about 400 bytes above the committed size, so any release that appended one more round tripped them; the §11.4.290 change set did not touch the preamble (0 mentions of §11.4.290 in it).
- **Fix: move, do not delete.** New tool `scripts/amendment_log/status_summary_archive.py` (`archive` and `verify`) moved every older cell entry, verbatim and in order, into [`docs/amendment-log/status-summary-claude.md`](docs/amendment-log/status-summary-claude.md) (64 entries, 140422 bytes), [`status-summary-agents.md`](docs/amendment-log/status-summary-agents.md) (60 entries, 133032 bytes) and [`status-summary-constitution.md`](docs/amendment-log/status-summary-constitution.md) (68 entries, 157730 bytes); each cell keeps its newest entry plus one pointer sentence naming the archive. Lossless proof: the archive header figures (entry count, moved bytes, sha256) are re-checked by `verify`, and an independent rebuild that does not import the tool (new cell + archive entries) reproduces the sha256 of the original row for all three files. Preambles are now 887 / 887 / 1505 bytes. Revisions: `CLAUDE.md` 84 to 85, `AGENTS.md` 81 to 82, `Constitution.md` 81 to 82 (last-modified fields updated). `constitution_index.yaml` regenerated (only `generated_at` and `source_sha256` changed; `groups/*.md` byte-identical), `progressive/catalog.json` and `progressive/rules_manifest.json` regenerated (only `source_sha256` changed; all 298 `progressive/rules/*.md` are byte-identical to a generation from the pre-change source). The tool's own test is `scripts/amendment_log/tests/test_status_summary_archive.sh` (16 checks on a synthetic carrier: independent lossless rebuild, newest-first order, rotation, idempotent second run, 6 refusal cases, only 3 carrier lines changed, plus 5 paired mutants of the tool that each must fail it). New amendments are recorded in this file, never appended to the cell; when the cell grows again, run `status_summary_archive.py archive --carrier <file> --archive docs/amendment-log/<file> --bump-revision`.
- **Parent repository (uncommitted here, owned by the main repository):** `docs/context/context_budget.conf` ratchets lowered to 1000 / 1000 / 1700 (and rows 1000 / 1000 added for `QWEN.md` / `GEMINI.md`, see the next bullet; `scripts/context_footprint.sh` lists both in `PREAMBLE_FILES`, without which a conf row is not enforced); `scripts/testing/test_context_footprint_preamble.sh` extended (39 checks: per-file ratchet row, real preamble, archive `verify`, a 6000-byte-growth mutant, an archive-re-inlined mutant and three archive-corruption mutants per file; 24 passed / 15 failed before the fix, 39 / 0 after).
- **`QWEN.md` and `GEMINI.md` moved the same way (2026-10-27, operator: "Fix it properly so no loss happens of any kind or the corruption").** They carried the same cell and had no ratchet row (preambles 133457 and 114572 bytes, measured at the time of the move; an earlier note here quoted 131654 and 113070 bytes, UNCONFIRMED what those figures measured). With the unchanged tool, the older entries went verbatim and in order to [`docs/amendment-log/status-summary-qwen.md`](docs/amendment-log/status-summary-qwen.md) (60 entries, 132887 bytes, sha256 `66be50f8e8a38d5b...`) and [`status-summary-gemini.md`](docs/amendment-log/status-summary-gemini.md) (43 entries, 113973 bytes, sha256 `f0a5417f2418ab9f...`); each cell keeps its newest entry (the 176-byte "Merge 2026-10-26" sentence) plus the pointer. Preambles are now 883 and 914 bytes; revisions `QWEN.md` 78 to 79, `GEMINI.md` 52 to 53 (`--bump-revision`). Lossless proof, by a script that does not import the tool: the pre-move `| Status summary |` row rebuilt from the new cell and the archive entries is byte-identical to the pre-move row (QWEN.md 133088 bytes sha256 `909426ee...0d18`, GEMINI.md 114174 bytes sha256 `c33c2b5e...cb48`), exactly two other lines differ (Revision +1, Last modified), the line count is unchanged, the part of each file below the preamble is byte-identical (`cmp`), and the three occurrences of §11.4.290 in each file (all below the preamble; none in the moved cell) are unchanged, the line holding them byte-identical. The script is now tracked and tested: `scripts/amendment_log/tests/independent_lossless_rebuild.py` (full mode needs the pre-move carrier; `--figures-only` re-checks the archive figures and the compact cell afterwards). The tool's own test grew from 16 to 39 checks (helper: golden-good, six negative fixtures, two usage-error cases and four paired mutants of the helper; per real carrier: verify, independent figure re-computation, §11.4.290 block-start exactly once and outside the preamble, idempotent re-run, five corruption mutants on copies); run against the pre-move snapshot with `CARRIER_ROOT=<dir>` the new section is red for the right reasons (16 failures: cell not compact, archive missing). Derived artifacts: `constitution_index.yaml`, `groups/*.md` and `progressive/*` are keyed on `Constitution.md` (`source_sha256`), which this move does not touch, so they needed no regeneration: `cm_constitution_index_fresh.sh` reports no drift and `progressive/generate_split.py --check` reports 300 artifacts byte-identical, both before and after. README row "Governance mirrors" now reads revisions 85 / 82 / 79 / 53. Parent repository: see the previous bullet; its ratchet test is 64 checks, 12 of them red before `PREAMBLE_FILES` was extended and the conf rows added.

### Honest boundaries

- The item-level seam is NOT shipped: the tracker `Verification:` field validation, the verification-job scheduler and the auto-reopen executor are each consuming project's own tracked work. The workable-items engine in this repository does not yet validate the `Verification:` line.
- The anchor does not relax the §11.4.236 / §11.4.115(F) / §11.4.135 seams. A dev release that must bypass the QA-deploy seam needs the §11.4.236(4) recorded override or a §11.4.271 waiver; whether the operator intends that is UNCONFIRMED.

Before the gate files were tracked `cm_gate_ledger_ratchet.sh` read unimplemented=395 against the unchanged baseline 394; with the four new gate files tracked (`cm_covenant_114_290_propagation.sh`, its mutation test, `cm_provisional_done_anchor_wired.sh`, its mutation test) it reads unimplemented=394, fails=0 (PASS, measured 2026-10-27 on the landing commit; the baseline was never raised).
